The above-mentioned contract is amended or modified as set forth in the following and these 
amendments and modifications of the plans and specifications are agreed to by the parties. All 
other aspects of the original agreement remain unchanged and in full force and effect. 
 
I. Description of Changes: 
This Modification adds additional compensation to the Agreement in an amount not to exceed 
Twenty-Five Thousand Dollars ($25,000.00) for continued on-call engineering services. The 
Consultant shall continue to provide services in accordance with the original Scope of Work, 
including preparation of design plans, construction support services, and other related 
engineering services as requested by the City, on an as-needed basis and subject to the 
availability of such funds. 
 
II. New Contract Items:  
None. 
 
III. Schedule:  
The Agreement term may extend beyond the original termination date set forth in Contract No. 
8218, if necessary, to allow for completion of services authorized under this Modification. Any 
such extension is limited to the period during which funds added by this Modification remain 
available and shall not exceed the time required to expend such funds. In no event shall the 
Contract continue after such funds have been fully expended or if the Agreement is otherwise 
terminated in accordance with its terms. 
 
IV. Summary: 
 
Original Contract Amount ...................................................................................$74,500.00

SUBJECT: 
Consideration of Bid #S-20-26 for a Contract between the City of 
Cheyenne and Richardson Construction, Inc. for the Cheyenne Botanic 
Gardens - Smith Education, Agriculture & Trial (E.A.T.) Garden 
 
Bid #S-20-26 was solicited for the Cheyenne Botanic Gardens - Smith Education, 
Agriculture & Trial (E.A.T.) Garden.  The Contractor will be completing grading, 
concrete foundation installation, garden bed installation, shade structure installation, 
fencing, and landscaping. 
 
Four (4) bidders responded to this bid, and the bid tabulation is attached.   
 
The Four (4) bids were received from: 
1. Big Horn Contracting, LLC 
2. EJ Services 
3. Reiman Corp. 
4. Richardson Construction, Inc. 
 
The bids were reviewed by RVi Planning + Landscape Architecture (Project Architect), 
Jason Sanchez (CRE), Sam Payne (CRE), and Aaron Sommers (Botanic Gardens), and 
the group recommends award to the lowest responsive bidder – Richardson 
Construction, Inc.   
 
The total requested bid award for is $854,892.21. 
 
Funding:   
Friends of the Botanic Gardens

CITY OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING 
AGREEMENT 
 
Bid No. S-20-26 
 
Contract No. _______ 
 
THIS AGREEMENT, entered into this ____ day of ___________, 2026, by and between 
the CITY OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING, hereinafter referred to as the “CITY”, and 
RICHARDSON CONSTRUCTION, INC., hereinafter referred to as the “CONTRACTOR”. 
 
WITNESSETH that the Contractor and the City, for the considerations stated herein, 
mutually agree as follows: 
 
ARTICLE 1.  STATEMENT OF WORK.  The Contractor shall furnish all supervision, 
technical personnel, labor, materials, machinery, tools, equipment, and services, including utility 
and transportation services, and perform and complete all work in an efficient and workmanlike 
manner in the construction of the “Cheyenne Botanic Gardens - Smith Education, Agriculture 
& Trial (E.A.T.) Garden” project all in strict accordance with the Contract Documents, including 
all addenda thereto, numbered and dated: Addendum One (1), dated March 17, 2026. 
 
ARTICLE 2.  SUMMARY OF WORK.   Contractor shall furnish all labor, materials, 
equipment, supervision, and services necessary to perform the Work in strict accordance with this 
Agreement and the Contract Documents identified in Article 6. The Work includes completing the 
Cheyenne Botanic Gardens - Smith Education, Agriculture & Trial (E.A.T.) Garden.  The 
Contractor will be completing grading, concrete foundation installation, garden bed installation, 
shade structure installation, fencing, and landscaping.  
 
ARTICLE 3.  RESPONSIBLE DESIGNEE FOR THE CITY.  The Contractor shall in any 
and all matters relating to the scope of services to be provided under this Contract, or any other 
provisions herein, contact the City Engineer, or his/her designated representative. 
 
ARTICLE 4.  TIME FRAME FOR COMPLETION.  The services to be performed under 
this Agreement shall commence on the date stipulated in the “Notice to Proceed” that will be issued 
by the City. The work shall be completed by December 31, 2026. If the work has not been 
completed within the time stipulated above, including any extensions of time issued by the City 
for excusable delays, the Contractor and his/her sureties shall pay the City fixed, agreed liquidated 
damages, as stipulated in the Supplemental Conditions, for each calendar day of delay until the 
work is completed. 
 
ARTICLE 5.  COMPENSATION AND METHOD OF PAYMENT.  The CITY will pay 
the CONTRACTOR for the performance of the Contract in current funds, for the Base Bid, a 
total not to exceed eight hundred fifty-one thousand and seven dollars, and twenty-one cents 
($851,007.21), and for the Additional Alternate #1, a total not to exceed three thousand eight 
hundred eighty-five dollars ($3,885.00), for a total contract cost not to exceed eight hundred 
fifty-four thousand, eight hundred ninety-two dollars, and twenty one cents ($854,892.21).  
In the event there are changes in the estimated quantities shown on the Bid Proposal, the unit prices 
8:48 am, Apr 08, 2026

Page 2 of 3 
multiplied by the actual quantities shall govern, and the total contract amount will be adjusted 
accordingly. The City agrees to pay the above amount for contractual services in the following 
manner, upon receipt of appropriate documentation: 
 
a. 
The Contractor will be paid on a monthly basis for the percentage of 
estimated work completed. Submittal will be at least seven (7) business days prior to the 
payable due date as established annually by the City Treasurer’s Office. The pay request 
shall be submitted on the Contract Payment Request Form and Itemized Pay Request or 
the AIA Documents G702 and G703. The engineer will review the estimate for approval 
prior to payment. 
 
b. 
The City will withhold five percent (5%) of the dollar value of the work 
completed for a minimum of forty-one (41) calendar days after Notice of Final Settlement 
has been published in accordance with Wyo. Stat. §16-6-116. Upon completion of the work 
under this Contract, the Contractor shall submit a Contractor’s Certificate of Completion; 
the Consent of Surety; Final Waivers of Lien from the Contractor, and all Sub-Contractors, 
Suppliers and Materialmen; Affidavit of Release of Liens; Affidavit of Payment; and a 
current Workers Compensation Certificate. Final payment will not be made until the above 
documents have been received by the City, all items on the Punch List have been 
completed, and the advertising requirements have been met. 
 
ARTICLE 6.  CONTRACT.  The executed Contract Documents shall consist of the 
following: 
 
1. This Agreement; 
2. Addenda; 
3. Invitation for Bids; 
4. Instructions to Bidders; 
5. Signed Bid Proposal; 
 
6. General Conditions and Insurance; 
7. Supplemental Conditions; 
8. Part IV Forms & Notices; 
9. Specifications and Special 
Provisions; and  
10. Drawings. 
 
This Agreement, together with other documents enumerated in this Article 6, which said 
other documents are as fully a part of the Contract as if hereto attached or herein repeated, forms 
the Contract between the parties hereto.

SUBJECT: 
Consideration of Bid #E-14-26 for furnishing Residential Automated 
Refuse Carts - 95/65 Gallons, for the Sanitation Division 
 
Four (4) bidders responded to this bid, and the bid tabulation is attached.   
 
The Four (4) bids were received from: 
1. Formex Holdings, LLC 
2. IPL North America Inc. (Rejected) 
3. Rehrig Pacific Company 
4. Schaefer Plastics North America, LLC (Rejected) 
 
The bids were reviewed by Austin Reiner (Sanitation), Craig LaVoy (Public Works), and 
Vicki Nemecek (Public Works).   
 
The bids submitted by Schaefer Plastics North America, LLC & IPL North America Inc., 
have been rejected, as their Refuse Carts did not meet all of the required specifications.   
 
Specifically:  
 
1. Their containers for the 65 gallon cart option did not meet the ‘actual volume’ 
requirement of 65 gallons.  Schaefer Plastics submitted a 64.5 gallon capacity 
cart, and IPL North America Inc. submitted a 64 gallon capacity cart.  Due to 
the bid requiring a 65 gallon volume capacity, these two bids have been 
rejected. 
 
Rehrig Pacific Company was the next lowest responsive bidder. 
 
The total requested bid award for Rehrig Pacific Company is $204,884.00.

MEMORANDUM

FROM:  
TJ Barttelbort, Purchasing Manager 
DATE:  
April 8, 2026 
SUBJECT: 
Consideration of Bid #E-15-26 for furnishing 1.5 Cubic Yard Rear-Load 
Dumpsters for the Sanitation Division 
 
Four (4) bidders responded to this bid, and the bid tabulation is attached.   
 
The Four (4) bids were received from: 
1. Fleet Innovations LLC. 
2. Kois Brothers Equipment 
3. Royal Media Network Inc. 
4. Wastequip Manufacturing Company LLC (Rejected) 
 
The bids were reviewed by Austin Reiner (Sanitation), Craig LaVoy (Public Works), and 
Vicki Nemecek (Public Works).   
 
The bid submitted by Wastequip Manufacturing Company LLC has been rejected, as 
their Rear-Load Container did not meet all of the required specifications.   
 
Specifically:  
 
1. Their container was 47.75” tall, instead of the maximum of 45” tall as called out 
in the specifications.   
2. Their washer on the trunnion bar to keep the container in place while it is being 
dumped was only 2.125”, and the specification required 3” minimum.  
 
Kois Bros Equipment was the next lowest responsive bidder. 
 
The total requested bid award for Kois Bros Equipment is $88,472.00.

REQUEST TO PURCHASE OVER $75,000.00 
Consideration to Purchase between the City of Cheyenne, and Musco Sports Lighting, LLC, for 
the purchase of a Mobile Video Trailer for the Community Recreation and Events Department.  
The total amount of the purchase is $161,826.00.  This is a single source purchase, therefore no 
bids were requested.   
 
This will be reviewed by the City Council Finance Committee on April 21, 2026 and voted on at 
the April 27, 2026 City Council meeting. 
 
TJ Barttelbort, Purchasing Manager 
City of Cheyenne, Wyoming 
(307) 773-1045 
 
Published in:  Wyoming Tribune-Eagle 
Dates: April 10, 17, 2026 

 
Page 1 of 2 
 
 
03/20/2026 
  
RE: Musco Sports Lighting, LLC (Musco) Sole Source  
  
Dear City of Cheyenne,   
 
We are pleased to present Musco’s MAX1710 Video Trailer to the City of Cheyenne. We set 
the standard in the LED display industry. American-made they deliver unmatched clarity and 
brightness.  
 
Our trailers are crafted in the Midwest and built to last.  With the help of Musco’s design 
team each trailer is engineered to our specific specifications. Musco is the sole source 
provider of the MAX 1710 trailer equipped with a production box with audio, speaker 
assembly, and a generator. The MAX1710 includes an industry leading warranty, and 24/7 
customer support. 
 
At Musco, our approach is more than just selling you a trailer. We are in the business of 
creating unforgettable moments. Client satisfaction is our number one priority. You become, 
not only a client, but also a partner.   
  
If you have questions or need additional information, please contact me by phone at (405) 
761-9134 by email at reese.smalley@musco.com.   
  
Thank you,   
  
Musco Sports Lighting, LLC  
211 2nd Ave West

Terms and Conditions
1. DESCRIPTION OF AGREEMENT. Insane Impact shall outline the sale of product(s) listed in attachment labelled (Final Invoice) and its deliverables to the 
Customer listed above with the equipment.
2. PAYMENT. Upon acceptance of signed Purchase Agreement, Insane Impact will provide a deposit invoice requiring a 60%Down/40% Due Upon Completion 
based on the total purchase price of the order. A ﬁnal invoice for the remaining balance will be due upon receipt of the product. Any government purchase 
order or pre-existing agreement in regard to payment terms will supersede Insane Impact, LLC's generic payment terms.
3. TERM. The term of this Agreement commences on the Effective Date and ends at the ﬁnal term, which is under warranty offering, pursuant to this 
Agreement (the “Term”).
4. OWNERSHIP. Customer shall keep all information conﬁdential including, but not limited to patents, trademarks, trade names, logos, corporate names, 
domain names, copyrights, trade secrets, and other intellectual property rights (collectively, the “Intellectual Property Rights”) therein.
5. WARRANTIES. Insane Impact (Seller) shall provide an agreed upon warranty to include parts and labor. The details and cost of this warranty will be outlined 
on the Final Invoice and provided in the “owners” manual which will accompany each product.
6. FORCE MAJEURE.
    a) Deﬁnition. For purposes of this Section, the term “Force Majeure” shall mean: (i) an act of God; (ii) ﬂood, ﬁre, earthquake, or explosion; (iii) war, invasion, 
terrorist threats or acts, or riots; (iv) government action, order, or law; (v) national or regional emergency; or (vi) for outdoor events, weather-related events, 
including, but not limited to, rain, lightning, hail, high wind, or snow.
    b) In the event of a “Force Majeure”, Insane Impact is NOT liable or responsible.
7. DEFAULT and REMEDIES.
• a) Events of Default. The following are “Events of Default”:
1.
Customer’s failure to pay the Balance upon delivery OR Insane Impact’s failure to deliver the product in accordance with the agreement outlined in the 
“Final Invoice.
2.
A party makes a general assignment for the beneﬁt of creditors or has a receiver, trustee, custodian, or similar agent appointed by order of any court of 
competent jurisdiction to take charge of or sell any material portion of its property or business.
• b) Insane Impact Default. Upon an Event of Default by Insane Impact, Customer may (i) terminate this Agreement upon written notice to Insane Impact; and/
or (ii) exercise any other available remedies at law or in equity.
• c) Customer Default. Upon an Event of Default by Customer, Insane Impact may terminate the Agreement immediately upon written notice to Customer and 
retain the Deposit as liquidated damages. The parties intend that these liquidated damages constitute compensation, and not a penalty. The parties 
acknowledge and agree that Insane Impact’s harm caused by Customer’s Event of Default would be impossible or very diﬃcult to accurately estimate as of 
the Effective Date, and that the liquidated damages are a reasonable estimate of the anticipated or actual harm that might arise from Customer’s Event of 
Default.
8. INDEMNIFICATION.
• a) Indemniﬁcation by Customer. Customer shall indemnify and hold Insane Impact and its oﬃcers, directors, employees, and agents harmless from and 
against any and all liabilities, costs, expenses, damages, claims, suits, actions, demands, and losses, without limitation, court costs and reasonable attorneys’ 
fees (collectively, the “Liabilities”) arising out of or resulting from any third party claim, suit, action, or proceeding (each, an “Action”) arising out of or resulting 
from:
1.
bodily injury, death of any person, or damage to real or tangible personal property resulting from the willful, fraudulent, or negligent acts or omissions 
of Customer or its oﬃcers, directors, employees, or agents; and
• b) Indemniﬁcation by Insane Impact. Insane Impact shall indemnify and hold Customer and its oﬃcers, directors, employees, and agents harmless from and 
against all Liabilities arising out of or resulting from any third-party Action arising out of or resulting from bodily injury, death of any person, or damage to 
real or tangible personal property resulting from the willful, fraudulent, or grossly negligent acts or omissions of Insane Impact or its oﬃcers, directors, 
employees, or agents.
9. ARBITRATION. Any controversies or disputes arising out of or relating to this Agreement will be resolved by binding arbitration in Des Moines, Iowa under 
the Iowa Arbitration Act, Chapter 679A of the Iowa Code. If the parties are unable to agree upon an arbitrator within thirty (30) days of written notice from a 
party requesting arbitration, the arbitrator will be appointed by the Iowa District Court for Polk County. The arbitrator's award will be ﬁnal, and judgment may 
be entered in the Iowa District Court for Polk County. The prevailing party shall be entitled to recover its expenses of arbitration, including reasonable 
attorney’s fees.
10. ENTIRE AGREEMENT. This Agreement contains the entire agreement of the parties, and there are no other promises or conditions in any other agreement 
whether oral or written concerning the subject matter of this Agreement. This Agreement supersedes any prior written or oral agreements between the 
parties.

11. SEVERABILITY. If any provision of this Agreement is held to be invalid or unenforceable for any reason, the remaining provisions will continue to be valid 
and enforceable. If a court ﬁnds that any provision of this Agreement is invalid or unenforceable, but that by limiting such provision it would become valid and 
enforceable, then such provision will be deemed to be written, construed, and enforced as so limited.
12. AMENDMENT. This Agreement may only be modiﬁed or amended by an agreement in writing signed by both parties.
13. GOVERNING LAW. This Agreement shall be construed in accordance with the laws of the State of Iowa without giving effect to any conﬂict or choice of law 
provision or rule (whether of the State of Iowa or any other jurisdiction).
14. NOTICE. Any notice or communication required or permitted under this Agreement shall be suﬃciently given if delivered in person or by certiﬁed mail, 
return receipt requested, to the address set forth in the opening paragraph or to such other address as one party may have furnished to the other in writing.
15. WAIVER. No waiver by any party of any of the provisions hereof shall be effective unless explicitly set forth in writing and signed by the party so waiving. The 
failure of either party to enforce any provision of this Agreement shall not be construed as a waiver or limitation of that party's right to subsequently enforce

SUBJECT: 
Consideration of Bid #S-19-26 for a Contract between the City of 
Cheyenne and Reiman Corp. for the Storey Boulevard Extension 
Construction project 
 
Bid #S-19-26 was solicited for the Storey Boulevard Extension Construction project.   
 
The Work includes completing the Storey Boulevard Extension from North College 
Drive east to Whitney Road. The Contractor will be completing demolition, grading, 
excavation, road construction, street surfacing, pavement markings, fencing, and 
landscaping. 
 
Five (5) bidders responded to this bid, and the bid tabulation is attached.   
 
The Five (5) bids were received from: 
1. Aztec Construction Co., Inc. 
2. JTL Group Inc. 
3. Reiman Corp. 
4. Simon Contractors 
5. S&S Builders, LLC 
 
The bids were reviewed by Tom Cobb (Engineering) and Cody Miller (Engineering), 
and the group recommends award to the lowest responsive bidder – Reiman Corp.  
 
The total requested bid award for is $3,493,630.85 
 
Funding: 
020-26-2616-42-43498 (1% Sales Tax 19-22)

THIS AGREEMENT, entered into this ____ day of ___________, 2026, by and between 
the CITY OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING, hereinafter referred to as the “CITY”, and REIMAN 
CORP., hereinafter referred to as the “CONTRACTOR”. 
 
WITNESSETH that the Contractor and the City, for the considerations stated herein, 
mutually agree as follows: 
 
ARTICLE 1.  STATEMENT OF WORK.  The Contractor shall furnish all supervision, 
technical personnel, labor, materials, machinery, tools, equipment, and services, including utility 
and transportation services, and perform and complete all work in an efficient and workmanlike 
manner in the construction of the “Storey Boulevard Extension Construction” project all in 
strict accordance with the Contract Documents, including all addenda thereto, numbered and 
dated: Addendum One (1), dated March 20, 2026, and Addendum Two (2), dated March 23, 
2026. 
 
ARTICLE 2.  SUMMARY OF WORK.  Contractor shall furnish all labor, materials, 
equipment, supervision, and services necessary to perform the Work in strict accordance with this 
Agreement and the Contract Documents identified in Article 6. The Work includes completing the 
Storey Boulevard Extension from North College Drive east to Whitney Road. The Contractor will 
be completing demolition, grading, excavation, road construction, street surfacing, pavement 
markings, fencing, and landscaping.  
 
ARTICLE 3.  RESPONSIBLE DESIGNEE FOR THE CITY.  The Contractor shall in any 
and all matters relating to the scope of services to be provided under this Contract, or any other 
provisions herein, contact the City Engineer, or his/her designated representative. 
 
ARTICLE 4.  TIME FRAME FOR COMPLETION.  The services to be performed under 
this Agreement shall commence on the date stipulated in the “Notice to Proceed” that will be issued 
by the City. The work shall be completed by December 31, 2026. If the work has not been 
completed within the time stipulated above, including any extensions of time issued by the City 
for excusable delays, the Contractor and his/her sureties shall pay the City fixed, agreed liquidated 
damages, as stipulated in the Supplemental Conditions, for each calendar day of delay until the 
work is completed. 
 
ARTICLE 5.  COMPENSATION AND METHOD OF PAYMENT.  The CITY will pay 
the CONTRACTOR for the performance of the Contract in current funds, a total not to exceed 
three million, four hundred ninety-three thousand, six hundred thirty dollars, and eighty-
five cents ($3,493,630.85). In the event there are changes in the estimated quantities shown on the 
Bid Proposal, the unit prices multiplied by the actual quantities shall govern, and the total contract

amount will be adjusted accordingly. The City agrees to pay the above amount for contractual 
services in the following manner, upon receipt of appropriate documentation: 
 
a. 
The Contractor will be paid on a monthly basis for the percentage of 
estimated work completed. Submittal will be at least seven (7) business days prior to the 
payable due date as established annually by the City Treasurer’s Office. The pay request 
shall be submitted on the Contract Payment Request Form and Itemized Pay Request or 
the AIA Documents G702 and G703. The engineer will review the estimate for approval 
prior to payment. 
 
b. 
The City will withhold five percent (5%) of the dollar value of the work 
completed for a minimum of forty-one (41) calendar days after Notice of Final Settlement 
has been published in accordance with Wyo. Stat. §16-6-116. Upon completion of the work 
under this Contract, the Contractor shall submit a Contractor’s Certificate of Completion; 
the Consent of Surety; Final Waivers of Lien from the Contractor, and all Sub-Contractors, 
Suppliers and Materialmen; Affidavit of Release of Liens; Affidavit of Payment; and a 
current Workers Compensation Certificate. Final payment will not be made until the above 
documents have been received by the City, all items on the Punch List have been 
completed, and the advertising requirements have been met. 
 
ARTICLE 6.  CONTRACT.  The executed Contract Documents shall consist of the

the Contract between the parties hereto.

SUBJECT: 
Consideration of Bid #S-21-26 for a Contract between the City of 
Cheyenne and Construction Specialists, Inc. for the Cheyenne Transit 
Facility - Interior and Exterior Renovations and Upgrades project 
 
Bid #S-21-26 was solicited for the Cheyenne Transit Facility - Interior and Exterior 
Renovations and Upgrades project. 
 
The Work includes completing the Cheyenne Transit Facility - Interior and Exterior 
Renovations and Upgrades.  The Contractor will be completing the interior renovation 
of the facility to allow for future use by the Cheyenne Transit Program.  Interior work 
includes drywall, electrical, plumbing and other interior finishes.  The Contractor will 
be completing exterior renovations to the parking lot and site to include paving, 
concrete work, curb and gutter work, and site grading. 
 
Four (4) bidders responded to this bid, and the bid tabulation is attached.  The Four 
(4) bids were received from: 
1. Big Horn Contracting, LLC 
2. Construction Specialists, Inc 
3. EJ Services 
4. Richardson Construction, Inc 
 
The bids were reviewed by Vicki Nemecek (Public Works), Craig LaVoy (Public Works), 
Adele Bartel (Public Works), and Erin Johnson (Transit), and the group recommends 
award to the lowest responsive bidder – Construction Specialists, Inc.  
 
The total requested bid award for is $2,169,500.00 
 
Funding: 
Federal Transit Administration Grant 5339 Funding, Bus and Facility 
Grant & WYDOT 5339 Federal Transit Administration Pass Through 
Grant.  ($2,005,800.00) 
 
Board of Public Utilities will be reimbursing the City for their portion.  
($163,700.00)

# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2026-03-17_TotalExtraction_84570064.pdf
RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY 
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE 
 
Office of City Clerk 
 
March 17, 2026 
 
 
The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne convened in a special meeting on this date 
beginning at 6:00 p.m. in City Council Chambers for the purpose of collective labor bargaining 
with the Cheyenne Professional Fire Fighters, International Association of Fire Fighters, Local 
No. 279.  Present were:  MAYOR – Patrick Collins; COUNCIL MEMBERS – Dr. Aldrich, Dr. 
Emmons, Mr. Esquibel, Mr. Laybourn, Mr. Moody, Mr. Segrave and Mr. White. Absent: Dr. Rinne 
and Mr. Wolfe. Also present: Kylie Soden, City Clerk; John Brodie, City Attorney; Amber Ash, 
Mayor’s Chief of Staff; Robin Lockman, City Treasurer and Chief Andrew Dykshorn, Deputy 
Chief Don Wood and Deputy Chief of Administrative Services, Glenn Rodgers, Cheyenne Fire 
Rescue.  The pledge of allegiance was recited. 
 
  
 
  
Chris Thoms, President, Local #279, advised the parties remain at an impasse regarding 
wages and expressed a preference to proceed with reviewing other sections of the contract. Mayor 
Collins emphasized the City’s goal of maintaining Cheyenne Fire Rescue as the best place to work. 
He reviewed current staffing levels, new fire stations, apparatus and equipment, and highlighted 
the City’s ongoing investments in firefighter safety. Mayor Collins expressed concern about losing 
momentum on progress made in negotiations and further reviewed compensation data, stating that 
Cheyenne firefighters are currently the highest paid in the state. He provided additional 
comparative information for Loveland, CO, including staffing levels, call volumes per station, 
number of stations, battalion chief assignments, as well as cost of living considerations and noted 
that Loveland is not unionized. He reiterated that wages remain the primary point of concern and 
expressed reluctance to move forward with other sections of the agreement at this time. Mr. Thoms 
requested a recess at 6:20 p.m., and Union members vacated chambers to the HR conference room 
to caucus. 
 
Mayor Collins reconvened the special session at 6:45 p.m. Mr. Thoms acknowledged that 
wages remain the primary issue and expressed the Union’s desire to continue moving forward with 
negotiations. Mayor Collins stated that the parties remain at a standstill pending agreement on 
certain key issues. Mr. Thoms requested that previously referenced data be provided for review in 
advance of the next meeting. 
 
 
There being no further business to come before the governing body, the meeting was 
officially adjourned at 6:49 p.m. 
 
 
 
 
 
 
Submitted by, 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
Kylie Soden, 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
City Clerk 
 
 
This notice is available in alternative, accessible formats upon request. 
 
Published: 
Wyoming Tribune-Eagle 
 
 
March 20, 2026 


--- END OF FILE ---


# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2026-03-16_TotalExtraction_47910712.pdf
RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY 
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE 
 
Office of City Clerk 
 
March 16, 2026 
 
 
The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne convened in a special meeting on this date 
beginning at 6:00 p.m. in City Council Chambers for the purpose of collective labor bargaining 
with the Cheyenne Professional Fire Fighters, International Association of Fire Fighters, Local 
No. 279.  Present were:  MAYOR – Patrick Collins; COUNCIL MEMBERS – Dr. Aldrich, Dr. 
Emmons, Mr. Esquibel, Mr. Laybourn, Mr. Moody, Mr. Segrave and Mr. White. Absent: Dr. Rinne 
and Mr. Wolfe. Also present: Kylie Soden, City Clerk; John Brodie, City Attorney; Amber Ash, 
Mayor’s Chief of Staff; Robin Lockman, City Treasurer and Chief Andrew Dykshorn, Deputy 
Chief Don Wood and Deputy Chief of Administrative Services, Glenn Rodgers, Cheyenne Fire 
Rescue.  The pledge of allegiance was recited. 
 
  
 
  
Chris Thoms, President of Local #279, emphasized the Union’s priority of maintaining fair 
market wages and noted that peer departments, including those along the Front Range, provide 
relevant comparisons. Jason Lindsay, Vice President of Local #279, presented pension and salary 
comparisons for firefighters in Wyoming, Colorado, and Montana. Discussion included specialty 
pay, Cheyenne’s economic growth, and cost-of-living data sourced from bestplaces.net. Mr. 
Thoms presented a counter proposal which included updates to Section 19 – Inclement Weather 
and Section 21 – Classification of Employment and Basic Wages. Additional discussion included 
recruitment efforts, attrition history, firefighter candidate pools, appropriate peer comparisons, the 
importance of aligning recurring revenues with recurring expenses, as well as the City’s use of 
reserves. 
 
Pursuant to Wyoming State Statute §16-4-405(a)(x), Mr. Segrave moved to meet in 
executive session, seconded by Mr. White.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the 
governing body present. The following staff members were in attendance: Amber Ash, Mayor’s 
Chief of Staff; John Brodie, City Attorney; Robin Lockman, City Treasurer; Kylie Soden, City 
Clerk and Chief Andrew Dykshorn, Deputy Chief Don Wood and Deputy Chief of Administrative 
Services, Glenn Rodgers, Cheyenne Fire Rescue. [City Clerk note: audio and video recording of 
the special meeting was stopped; Executive Session convened at 6:41 p.m. in the City Council 
office] 
 
Following closure of the executive session, Mayor Collins reconvened the special meeting 
at 7:06 p.m. and discussion continued regarding wage comparisons, specialty pay, and appropriate 
peer groups, including how comparable communities structure and fund compensation. Mayor 
Collins expressed the belief that the City’s firefighters are not underpaid. Discussion also included 
cost-of-living comparisons among certain Wyoming communities, paramedic compensation and 
training considerations, and ongoing challenges in reaching an agreement on wage comparisons 
and the identification of appropriate peer groups. The City reiterated its standing offer related to 
wage increases. The Union requested adjournment to review rank average amounts provided by 
the City. 
 
 
There being no further business to come before the governing body, the meeting was 
officially adjourned at 7:35 p.m. 

 
 
 
 
 
 
Submitted by, 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
Kylie Soden, 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
City Clerk 
 
 
This notice is available in alternative, accessible formats upon request. 
 
Published: 
Wyoming Tribune-Eagle 
 
 
March 20, 2026 
  


--- END OF FILE ---


# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2026-02-03_TotalExtraction_36052295.pdf
   
RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY 
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE 
Office of City Clerk 
 
February 23, 2026 
 
 
The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in special session on this date beginning 
at 5:30 p.m. in Council Chambers and via electronic conference meeting, for the purpose of 
collective labor bargaining with the Cheyenne Professional Firefighters, International Association 
of Firefighters, Local No. 279.  Present were: MAYOR – Patrick Collins, COUNCIL MEMBERS 
– Dr. Michelle Aldrich, Dr. Kathy Emmons, Ken Esquibel, Pete Laybourn, Mark Moody, Dr. Mark 
Rinne, Tom Segrave (via Zoom), Jeff White and Lawrence Wolfe.  Also present: Kylie Soden, 
City Clerk, Amber Ash, Chief of Staff, John Brodie, City Attorney, Robin Lockman, City 
Treasurer and Chief Andrew Dykshorn, Deputy Chief Don Wood and Deputy Chief of 
Administrative Services, Glenn Rodgers, Cheyenne Fire Rescue. The pledge of allegiance was 
recited. 
 
Mayor Collins provided opening remarks and emphasized the importance of the labor-
management discussions that take place in advance of formal negotiations. The proposed Ground 
Rules were reviewed, agreed upon, and signed by both the City and the Union. [Clerk’s Note: The 
City’s spokespersons are Mayor Patrick Collins, City Attorney John Brodie, and Chief of Staff 
Amber Ash. The Union’s spokespersons are Union President Chris Thoms, Union Vice President 
Jason Lindsay, and Union Grievance Representative James Lewis.]  
 
Mayor  Collins announced that additional negotiation meetings will be scheduled in the 
coming weeks to review City and Union proposals. 
 
  
  
There being no further business to come before the governing body, the meeting was 
officially adjourned at 5:33 p.m. 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
Submitted by, 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
Kylie Soden, City Clerk

Wyoming Tribune-Eagle

# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2026-01-06_TotalExtraction_35141854.pdf
 
Page 1 of 4 
 
ORDINANCE NO. ________ 
 
ENTITLED: "AN ORDINANCE CREATING CHAPTER 1.28, ADMINISTRATIVE 
INSPECTION WARRANTS, ESTABLISHING AUTHORITY FOR THEIR 
ISSUANCE, AND AMENDING THE MUNICIPAL CODE OF THE CITY 
OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING, TO AUTHORIZE CERTAIN CITY 
OFFICIALS, AS SPECIFIED, TO APPLY FOR ADMINISTRATIVE 
INSPECTION WARRANTS." 
 
 
BE IT ORDAINED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE, 
WYOMING: 
 
 
Section 1.  That Chapter 1.28, Administrative Inspection Warrants, of Title 1, General 
Provisions, and Section 2.32.050 – Administrative Inspection Warrants—Fire Department 
Authority, of Chapter 2.32 – Fire Department, of Title 2 – Administration and Personnel, of the 
municipal code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, are hereby created to read: 
 
Chapter 1.28 – ADMINISTRATIVE INSPECTION WARRANTS 
 
1.28.010 – Issuance of administrative inspection warrants. 
 
A. A municipal judge may, upon proper application of a city official or a city employee so 
authorized, issue an administrative inspection warrant if such inspection is authorized, required, 
or reasonably necessary under any ordinance or other law of the City of Cheyenne or the State of 
Wyoming and the issuance of the warrant otherwise complies with the requirements imposed under 
this Chapter. 
 
B. An administrative inspection warrant shall be in the form of a written order particularly 
describing the property or premises to be entered, the purpose and scope of the inspection, and the 
legal authority under which the warrant is issued. 
 
C. The warrant shall authorize entry onto the designated property at reasonable times and in 
a reasonable manner for the purposes of conducting the inspection or investigation specified 
therein. 
 
D. Nothing in this section shall be construed to authorize the issuance of an administrative 
inspection warrant in a manner inconsistent with the Constitution or laws of the United States or 
the State of Wyoming. 
 
1.28.020 – Grounds for issuance. 
 
A. An administrative inspection warrant shall be issued only upon cause, supported by 
affidavit or sworn statement made under oath or affirmation, particularly describing: 
 
1. The applicant’s authority and status in applying for the warrant; 
 
9:28 am, Dec 16 2025

 
Page 2 of 4 
 
2. The ordinance, statute or regulation requiring or authorizing the inspection or 
investigation; 
 
3. The premises to be inspected or investigated; and 
 
4. The purpose for the inspection or investigation, including the basis upon which cause 
exists to inspect or investigate. 
 
B. In addition to the requirements imposed under subsection (A) of this section, the affidavit 
or sworn statement shall include a declaration that entry has been sought and refused, that 
a reasonable attempt has been made to obtain consent and the attempt has been 
unsuccessful or facts demonstrating that the purpose of the inspection or investigation 
might be frustrated if prior consent were sought before application for a warrant. 
 
C. Cause shall be deemed to exist if: 
 
1. Reasonable legislative or administrative standards for conducting a routine, periodic or 
area inspection are satisfied with respect to the particular premises; 
 
2. There is probable cause to believe that a condition of nonconformity, violation or other 
circumstance requiring corrective action exists with respect to the designated property; 
or 
 
3. An inspection or investigation is reasonably necessary to determine, discover or verify 
the condition of the property, structures or operations for compliance with applicable 
law or regulation. 
 
1.28.030 – Procedure for issuance. 
 
A. Before issuing an administrative inspection warrant, the municipal judge may examine, 
under oath or affirmation, the applicant and any other witnesses the judge deems necessary 
and shall determine from the evidence presented whether sufficient grounds exist for 
granting the application. 
 
B. If the municipal judge finds that cause exists for the inspection or investigation and that all 
other requirements of this chapter have been satisfied, the judge shall issue the warrant. 
The warrant shall particularly describe the name and title of the person or persons 
authorized to execute it, the premises or property to be entered, and the purpose or purposes 
of the inspection and investigation.  
 
C. The warrant shall direct that it be executed only during reasonable hours and in a reasonable 
manner, except that where the municipal judge makes a specific finding that effective 
execution cannot be accomplished during those hours, the judge may authorize execution 
at such other times as the judge determines appropriate. 
 

 
Page 3 of 4 
 
D. The municipal judge shall retain the application, supporting affidavit or sworn statement, 
and a copy of the warrant as part of the official record of the proceeding. 
 
1.28.040 – Execution of administrative inspection warrants. 
 
A. Except as provided in Section 1.28.020(B), in executing an administrative inspection 
warrant, the person authorized to execute the warrant shall, before entry, make a reasonable 
effort to present their credentials and state their authority and purpose to any occupant or 
person in possession of the premises designated in the warrant and shall, upon request, 
display a copy of the warrant. 
 
B. In executing an administrative inspection warrant, the person authorized to execute the 
warrant shall not be required to provide prior notice of authority or purpose as required in 
subsection (A) of this section if the premises are unoccupied, are not in the possession of 
any person, or are reasonably believed, at the time of entry, to be in such condition that 
providing prior notice would frustrate the purpose of the inspection or investigation. 
 
C. A peace officer or other law enforcement official may be requested to accompany or assist 
in the execution of an administrative inspection warrant when reasonably necessary to 
ensure the safety of personnel or the security of the premises. 
 
D. An administrative inspection warrant shall be executed and returned to the municipal judge 
within ten (10) days from its date of issuance, unless the judge, before the expiration of the 
ten (10) day period, extends the time for execution by written endorsement for an additional 
five (5) days. Any warrant not executed within the prescribed time shall be void. 
 
2.32.050 - Administrative inspection warrants—Fire department authority. 
 
The chief of the fire department, or an authorized designee, may request the issuance of 
administrative inspection warrants in accordance with Chapter 1.28 of the city code when 
necessary to enforce or ensure compliance with the International Fire Code adopted by Chapter 
8.12 of the city code, the applicable provisions of Chapters 8.16, 8.20, 8.24, 8.32, and 8.40 of the 
city code, and Wyoming Statutes §§ 35-9-109, 35-9-111, 35-9-112, 35-9-116, and 35-9-156. 
 
Section 2.  That Section 2.36.010, Administration – Authority of staff, of Chapter 2.36, 
Building Safety Department, of Title 2, Administration and Personnel, of the City of Cheyenne, 
Wyoming, is hereby amended to read: 
 
2.36.010 - Administration—Authority of staff. 
 
A. The city's chief building official is the chief administrative and enforcement officer of the 
building safety department. The chief building official, is the individual occupying the 
position of chief building official, also referred to as code official. The chief building 
official is hereby authorized and directed to interpret and enforce also referred to as the 
code official, is responsible for interpreting and enforcing the provisions of Chapters 15.04 
through 15.48, and Chapter 5.44 of this code. The chief building official shall is hereby 

 
Page 4 of 4 
 
authorized and directed to administer and enforce these provisions, to issue necessary any 
notices and/or or orders necessary to ensure compliance, with this title. and to request the 
issuance of administrative inspection warrants as provided in Chapter 1.28 of this code, 
when such warrants are necessary to perform inspections or investigations authorized under 
this title. 
 
B. Building safety department staff, under the direction of the chief building official, will 
perform all plan review and building inspection functions including, but not limited to, 
issuing building, electrical, plumbing, mechanical and other permits pertinent to building 
construction, whether public or private buildings; compile affiliated building records, and 
ensure the collection of plan review, building permit, inspection and related fees. 
 
 
Section 3.  Sections, paragraphs, sentences, clauses, and phrases of this ordinance are 
severable. If any section, paragraph, sentence, clause, or phrase of this ordinance is declared 
unconstitutional or otherwise unlawful, such declaration shall not affect any of the remaining 
sections, paragraphs, sentences, clauses, and phrases. 
 
 
Section 4.  This ordinance shall be in full force and effect upon approval and publication.

# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2026-01-06_TotalExtraction_22957938.pdf
 
www.cheyennefire.org 
307-637-6311 
 
 
 
Policy Brief for the Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne 
Date: 1-8-2026 
Subject: City of Cheyenne – Administrative Inspection Warrants (Chapter 1.28) 
Purpose: 
Establishes a straightforward, constitutional process for city officials to obtain warrants for 
inspections or investigations when consent is denied, or cannot be obtained, ensuring compliance 
with municipal and state law while maintaining constitutional rights of property owners. 
Key Provisions: 
1. When is a Warrant Needed?  
CFR would request an administrative inspection warrant for the following actions when 
access is denied or cannot be obtained: 
• 
Fire Code Inspections: (For Commercial Properties) 
o Routine or follow-up inspections when consent is refused to ensure 
compliance with the International Fire Code adopted by the City of Cheyenne. 
• 
Fire Investigations: (Post-incident Residential and Commercial Properties) 
o Determining cause and origin after a fire when property access when legal 
access to the property cannot be obtained through consent. 
o If, during the course of an investigation, evidence of criminal activity is 
discovered, all investigations will halt, and a criminal search warrant will be 
obtained in coordination with law enforcement. 
2. Authority to Issue  
• 
A municipal judge may issue an administrative inspection warrant upon proper 
application by an authorized city official or employee.  
• 
Warrants must comply with the U.S. and Wyoming Constitutions. 
(§1.28.010) 
3. Warrant Requirements:  
• 
Written order describing: 

 
www.cheyennefire.org 
307-637-6311 
 
 
 
o Property or premises to be entered.  
o Purpose and scope of inspection.  
o Legal authority for issuance with documented length of time for access.  
(§1.28.010(B))  
4. Grounds for Issuance: 
• 
Supported by affidavit under oath, including: 
o Applicant’s authority.  
o Applicable ordinance or statute.  
o Description of premises and purpose.  
• 
Must show: 
o Consent was sought and refused, or  
o Consent would frustrate the inspection purpose, or consent could not be obtained.  
• 
Cause exists if: 
o Fire investigation incomplete and further investigation needed to determine the 
causation of the fire, OR 
o Routine inspection standards apply, OR  
o Probable life safety hazard exists, OR  
o Inspection needed to verify compliance. 
(§1.28.020)  
5. Procedure: 
• 
Application submitted by the Fire Chief, or authorized designee, submits a sworn 
statement. 
• 
Judge reviews evidence and may examine the applicant under oath.  
• 
Warrant specifies authorized personnel, premises, and purpose.  
• 
Executed during reasonable hours, unless the judge authorizes otherwise. 
(§1.28.030)  

 
www.cheyennefire.org 
307-637-6311 
 
 
 
6. Execution:  
• 
Show credentials and warrant before entry (unless notice frustrates purpose).  
• 
Law enforcement may assist for safety.  
• 
Must be executed within 10 days (extendable by 5 days). 
(§1.28.040)  
Fire Department Authority: 
• 
Fire Chief, or designee, may request warrants to enforce the adopted International Fire 
Code (IFC 104.4: Right of Entry & 104.4.1: Warrant) and Wyoming Statutes (§§359109, 
111, 112, 116, 156). 
(§2.32.050)  
Key Case Law 
• 
Michigan v. Tyler (1978) & Michigan v. Clifford (1984): (Fire Investigations) 
o Warrantless searches after an emergency ends violate the 4th Amendment. 
▪ 
U.S. Supreme Court recommends utilizing the Administrative Warrant 
Process 
o Evidence obtained without a proper warrant is inadmissible. 
• 
Camara v. Municipal Court: (Commercial Occupancy Fire Inspections) 
o Administrative inspection searches require a warrant unless consent is given. 
Why It Matters: 
• Protects constitutional rights.  
• Enables enforcement of fire code in commercial occupancies.  
• Accurately determine the cause and origin of fires to help prevent them from occurring in 
the future. 
• Provides a transparent, legal process for inspections when consent is denied or cannot be 
obtained.  
• Ensures compliance with state statutes, the adopted international fire code, and municipal 
code. 


--- END OF FILE ---


# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2026-01-06_TotalExtraction_34200017.pdf
PLANNING & 
DEVELOPMENT 
2101 O’Neil Avenue 
Cheyenne, WY 82001 
(307) 637-6282 
cheyennecity.org 
 
MEMO 
To: 
Honorable Mayor Patrick Collins and Members of the City Council 
From: 
Connor White 
Subject: PUDC-25-138 County Pocket Annexation #9 – Annexation 
Date: 
January 12, 2026 
______________________________________________________________________________________________________________ 
 
Background:  
 
The item for consideration is an Ordinance. Annexation of County pockets is a 2022 City 
Council priority.  A “County pocket” is an area of unincorporated land 100% surrounded by 
the City of Cheyenne.  A related “Assigned Zoning” pursuant to Unified Development Code 
(UDC) Section 2.2.8 ordinance is also on the agenda.    
 
The proposed Governing Body review schedule is as follows: 
• 
Introduction and Referral  
January 12, 2026 
• 
Public Services Committee  
January 20, 2026 
• 
Second reading  
 
January 26, 2026 
• 
Public Services Committee  
February 2, 2026 
• 
Third and final reading   
February 9, 2026 
 
This annexation is comprised of 26 properties 100% surrounded by the City (See attached 
map). Of the 26 parcels, all are undeveloped or incorporated into adjacent parcels under 
similar ownership. The City has maintained contact with the County Planning Office to 
coordinate any pending permits or development reviews.  Currently there are no active 
permits or development specific to the properties proposed for annexation.   
 
The existing use of any property annexed, including any livestock use, will be allowed to 
continue as is as a nonconforming use.  Nonconforming uses may continue until they are 
abandoned for a period of 12-months. Residents within pockets have been encouraged to 
complete a Land Use Documentation form detailing the existing use of the land for 
documentation purposes.  At the time of drafting this memo, two property owner within the 
area have completed a land use documentation form. Providing a completed form is not 
required nor does it prohibit a property owner from making future claims that a use was pre-
exiting.  The form is simply a tool to assist future City staff members to determine if a use 
was in existence at the time of annexation.   
 
City-initiated annexations do not require an owner’s signature. To initiate an annexation, the 
City must adopt a resolution certifying that the annexation complies with Wyoming State 
Statute § 15-1-402. After the City adopts this resolution, the annexation follows the typical 
schedule, including a public hearing and the approval of an Ordinance which annexes the 
land. The Governing Body adopted Resolution No. 6504 initiating this annexation on 
December 22, 2025. 
 

 
As with any annexation, City services will be extended to service the land contained in the 
annexation. City utilities are already located in the vicinity. Police, fire, and sanitation services 
will be provided. Infrastructure improvements required related to the annexation are minimal. 
Future development such as subdivision or new development will require on- and off-site 
infrastructure improvements. Existing infrastructure will remain as is upon annexation.  
 
The areas proposed for annexation are adjacent to land under City jurisdiction. Development 
in this area is a natural extension of the City. 100 % of the perimeter of the annexation area is 
contiguous to City limits.   
 
Due to meeting the review criteria found in Wyoming Statute § 15-1-402 and the annexation 
conforming with the 2022 City Council goal to annex County pockets, staff recommends 
approval of this annexation. 
 
Staff Recommendation: 
 
Staff recommends the Governing Body approve the Annexation Ordinance.  
 
Attachments: 
1. Proposed Ordinance (includes Annexation Map as Attachment A) 
2. Staff Report

(c) 
That the annexation of said areas is for the protection of health, safety, and welfare 
of the persons residing in the areas and the City of Cheyenne; 
 
(d) 
That the urban development of the areas sought to be annexed will constitute a 
natural, geographical, economical and social part of the City of Cheyenne; 
 
(e) 
That the areas are a logical and feasible addition to the City and that the extension 
of basic services such as water and sewer systems, police and fire protection, and 
other services customarily available in the City of Cheyenne can, within reason, be 
furnished; 
 
(f) 
That the areas are considered contiguous to the City of Cheyenne according to the 
provisions of Wyo. Stat. § 15-1-402(a)(iv) and (b) and is currently zoned LU Land 
Use by the Laramie County Commissioners; and 
 
(g) 
That the City of Cheyenne does not own or operate its own electrical utility, and its 
governing body is prepared to designate a utility to serve the annexed areas pursuant 
to Wyo. Stat. § 15-1-410, and to authorize the designated utility to serve the entirety 
of the annexed areas. 
 
Section 2.  That pursuant to Wyo. Stat. § 15-1-405, the Governing Body held a public 
hearing on January 12, 2026, on the proposed annexation and found that the annexation complied 
Wyo. Stat. § 15-1-402. 
 
Section 3.  That the map of the areas to be annexed is shown in Exhibit A, which is attached 
hereto and incorporated herein, satisfies the requirements of Wyo. Stat. § 15-1-406, and shall be 
recorded with the Laramie County Clerk in conjunction with this Ordinance. 
 
Section 4.  That all of the real property described in Section 1(b) of this Ordinance is hereby 
annexed to the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, and shall become a part thereof.  
 
Section 5.  That the City Clerk is directed to file an executed copy of this annexation 
ordinance with the County Clerk and Ex-Officio Register of Deeds for Laramie County, Wyoming, 
and shall also notify the City Engineer's Office to add said area to the official City Map of the City 
of Cheyenne, Wyoming. 
 
Section 6.  Sections, paragraphs, sentences, clauses and phrases of this ordinance are 
severable. If any phrase, clause, sentence, paragraph or section of this ordinance is declared illegal 
or unconstitutional, such illegality or unconstitutionality shall not affect any of the remaining 
phrases, clauses, sentences, paragraphs and sections.  
 

Section 7.  That this ordinance shall be in full force and effect upon its publication.

CITY OF CHEYENNE GOVERNING BODY 
JANUARY 12, 2026 
STAFF REPORT 
 
FILE: 
PUDC-25-258: County Pocket #9 - Annexation 
REQUEST: 
An annexation of 26 properties 100% surrounded by the City. 
LOCATION: 
South of Storey Boulevard, east of Powderhouse Road, north of Dell 
Range Boulevard, and west of Ridge Road. 
PURPOSE: 
Annexation of County pockets is a 2022 City Council priority. 
PREPARED BY: 
Connor White, Planner II 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 

PUDC-25-258 
 
 
January 12, 2026 
2 | P a g e 
APPLICABLE CITY CODE SECTION(S) AND PLANS: 
 
• 
UDC 2.4.2 Annexations 
• 
PlanCheyenne 
 
BACKGROUND: 
 
Annexation of County pockets is a 2022 City Council priority.  A “County pocket” is an area of unincorporated 
land 100% surrounded by the City of Cheyenne. City-initiated annexations do not require an owner’s 
signature. To initiate an annexation, the City must adopt a resolution certifying that the annexation 
complies with Wyoming State Statute § 15-1-402. After the City adopts this resolution, the annexation 
follows the typical schedule, including a public hearing and the approval of an Ordinance which annexes 
the land. The Governing Body adopted Resolution No. 6504 initiating this annexation on December 22, 
2025. 
 
This annexation is comprised of 26 properties 100% surrounded by the City (See attached map). Of the 26 
parcels, all parcels are undeveloped or incorporated into adjacent parcels under similar ownership. 
 
Land annexed to the City requires a City zone. An assigned zoning ordinance is accompanying this annexation 
to assign the AG and MR zones.  
 
ANALYSIS OF APPLICATION CONFORMANCE WITH REVIEW CRITERIA: 
 
To approve the annexation Ordinance, the Governing Body must make the following findings required in 
WS 15-1-402; a detailed analysis of which is below: 
 
1) An annexation of the area is for the protection of the health, safety and welfare of 
the persons residing in the area and in the city or town.  [WS 15-1-402 (a) (i)] 
 
The annexation of these properties will increase the health, safety, and welfare of the future 
occupants. By being in City limits, the property will receive access to City Fire and Police and 
sanitation services. The properties will also have the option of connecting to City water and 
sanitary sewer instead of having wells and septic systems.  
 
The application complies with this review criterion. 
 
2) The urban development of the area sought to be annexed would constitute a 
natural, geographical, economical and social part of the annexing city or town. [WS 
15-1-402 (a) (ii)] 
 
These properties constitute a natural, geographic, economic, and social part of the city. The 
properties are surrounded by City limits on all sides. The contiguity to the City and the proximity 
to surrounding urban-style development makes the parcels a natural geographic part of the City.  
 
The application complies with this review criterion. 
 
 
  

PUDC-25-258 
 
 
January 12, 2026 
3 | P a g e 
3) The area sought to be annexed is a logical and feasible addition to the annexing 
city or town and the extension of basic and other services customarily available to 
residents of the city or town shall, within reason, be available to the area proposed 
to be annexed. [WS 15-1-402 (a) (iii)] 
 
The area is a logical and feasible addition to the City due to the reasons previously listed. All City 
services normally available to residents will also be made available. Police and fire protection will 
be extended upon annexation. Utilities exist in the area and will be expanded into the proposed 
area as they redevelop.  
 
The application complies with this review criterion. 
 
4) The area sought to be annexed is contiguous with or adjacent to the annexing city 
or town, or the area meets the requirements of W.S. 15-1-407. [WS 15-1-402 (a) 
(iv)] 
 
The area proposed for annexation is 100% contiguous on all sides.   
 
The application complies with this review criterion. 
 
5) If the city or town does not own or operate its own electric utility, its governing 
body is prepared to issue one (1) or more franchises as necessary to serve the 
annexed area pursuant to W.S. 15-1-410. [WS 15-1-402 (a) (v)] 
 
Utility franchises are in place to serve the annexed area.   
 
The application complies with this review criterion. 
    
6) The annexing city or town, not less than twenty (20) business days prior to the 
public hearing required by W.S. 15-1-405(a), has sent by certified mail to all 
landowners and affected public utilities within the territory and by first class mail 
to any persons owning property that is adjacent to or within three hundred (300) 
feet of the territory proposed to be annexed, regardless of whether the property is 
inside or outside the corporate limits of the annexing city or town and regardless 
of whether the city or town is exercising authority under W.S. 15-3-202(b)(ii), a 
summary of the proposed annexation report as required under subsection (c) of 
this section and notice of the time, date and location of the public hearing required 
by W.S. 15-1-405(a). [WS 15-1-402 (a) (vi)] 
 
A certified mailing was made to the landowners and public utilities on December 5, 2025. 
Neighbors within 300’ were mailed first-class letters the same day. The public hearing for this 
annexation is scheduled for January 12, 2026. Twenty (20) business days (on the City’s calendar) 
prior to the announced public hearing is December 9, 2025. The earliest confirmation of delivery 
was dated December 8, 2025. 
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   
 

PUDC-25-258 
 
 
January 12, 2026 
4 | P a g e 
CONFORMANCE WITH APPLICABLE PLANS: 
 
The project is located in an area covered by the following plans: 
• 
Plan Cheyenne 
 
The Future Land Use Map of PlanCheyenne, as updated by Connect 2045, anticipates this area of the 
community to develop with Urban Residential uses and a little bit of Mixed-use Commercial uses. These 
areas are expected to develop with full access to urban services – such areas are best served by being 
within City limits. The area is within the Urban Service Boundary. 
 
STAFF REVIEW: 
 
Through the review process, no comments were received. 
 
CONCLUSION: 
 
Staff is satisfied that the application meets the review criteria and therefore recommends approval of the 
annexation.   
 
The proposed annexation is a natural extension of the City, is contiguous to the current City limits, and 
most services typically available to urban areas are available to be extended to this property.  
 
STAFF RECOMMENDATION: 
 
Staff recommends approval of the annexation. 
 
ATTACHMENTS: 
1. Current Zoning Map 
2. Future Land Use Map 
3. Annexation map

From: 
Connor White 
Subject: PUDC-25-258 County Pocket Annexation #9 – Assigned Zoning  
Date: 
January 12, 2026 
______________________________________________________________________________________________________________ 
 
Background:  
The item for consideration is an Assigned Zoning. This is approved by Ordinance.    
 
The proposed Governing Body review schedule is as follows: 
• 
Introduction and Referral   
January 12, 2026 
• 
Public Services Committee  
January 20, 2026 
• 
Second reading   
 
January 26, 2026 
• 
Public Services Committee  
February 2, 2026 
• 
Third and final reading  
 
February 9, 2026 
 
Planning Commission Recommendation:  
The Planning Commission heard this item at their January 5, 2026 meeting and recommended the 
Governing Body approve this application.  
 
The Planning Commission Staff report is attached and has not been changed. 
 
Attachments: 
1. 
Ordinance 
2. 
Planning Commission Staff Report 
 
 

ORDINANCE NO. ________ 
 
ENTITLED: “AN ORDINANCE AMENDING THE OFFICIAL ZONING MAP OF THE 
CITY 
OF 
CHEYENNE, 
ESTABLISHING 
THE 
ZONING 
CLASSIFICATIONS OF AG – AGRICULTURAL, AND MR - MEDIUM-
DENSITY RESIDENTIAL, FOR LAND ANNEXED TO THE CITY OF 
CHEYENNE 
GENERALLY 
LOCATED 
SOUTH 
OF 
STOREY 
BOULEVARD, EAST OF POWDERHOUSE ROAD, NORTH OF DELL 
RANGE BOULEVARD, AND WEST OF RIDGE ROAD.” 
 
 
BE IT ORDAINED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE, 
WYOMING: 
 
 
Section 1.  That in accordance with the calendar year 2022 goal of the Governing Body, 
City staff have petitioned the Governing Body for a zoning map amendment (zone change) for 
property generally located south of Storey Boulevard, east of Powderhouse Road, north of Dell 
Range Boulevard, and west of Ridge Road in accordance with Section 2.2.1, Zoning Map 
Amendment, of the Cheyenne Unified Development Code ("UDC"). 
 
 
Section 2.  That Section 2.2.8 of the UDC provides standards and procedures for zoning 
districts established on land or property currently without a City zone assignment. 
 
 
Section 3.  That on January 5, 2026, the City of Cheyenne Planning Commission conducted 
a public hearing, reviewed the proposed zone changes that are the subject of this ordinance, and 
recommended approval of said zone changes. 
 
 
Section 4.  That the Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne finds that the zone changes 
that are the subject of this ordinance meet the criteria specified in Section 2.2.8.d of the UDC. 
 
 
Section 5.  That, in accordance with Section 2.2.8, Assigned Zoning, Section 5.1.2, Zoning 
Districts Established, and Section 5.1.3, Official Zoning Map, of the UDC, the zoning 
classifications of the real properties particularly described below are hereby established as follows: 
Pocket A zoned County LU – Land Use is zoned AG – Agricultural, Pocket B zoned County LU 
– Land Use is zoned MR – Medium-density Residential, and Pocket C zoned County LU – Land 
Use is zoned MR – Medium-density Residential:

Section 6.  That the City Engineer is hereby authorized and directed to alter and amend the 
Official Zoning Map as described in Section 5.1.3 of the UDC to comply with the zone change as 
described in Section 5 above upon the effective date of this Ordinance as specified by Section 8. 
 
 
Section 7.  Sections, paragraphs, sentences, clauses and phrases of this ordinance are 
severable. If any section, paragraph, sentence, clause, or phrase of this ordinance is declared 
unconstitutional, such unconstitutionality shall not affect any of the remaining sections, 
paragraphs, sentences, clauses, and phrases. 
 
 
Section 8.  That this ordinance shall be in full force and effect upon its approval and 
publication.

Wyoming Tribune-Eagle 

 
1 | P a g e 
 
 
 
 
 
CITY OF CHEYENNE PLANNING COMMISSION 
JANUARY 5, 2026 
STAFF REPORT 
 
FILE: 
 
PUDC-25-258: County Pocket Annexation #9. – Assigned 
Zoning 
LOCATION: 
South of Storey Boulevard, east of Powderhouse Road, North of 
Dell Range Boulevard, and west of Ridge Road.   
REQUEST: 
Assigning City zones to properties that the City is annexing. 
ANNEXATION 
APPLICANT: 
City of Cheyenne 
OWNER: 
Various 
CURRENT ZONING: 
County LU  
PROPOSED ZONING: 
AG and MR. 
FUTURE LAND USE: 
Urban Residential and Mixed-use Commercial.  
PURPOSE: 
To establish a City zoning district concurrent with annexation. 
PREPARED BY: 
Connor White, Planner II 
 

PUDC-25-258 
 
 
January 5, 2026 
2 | P a g e 
 
 
RECOMMENDED MOTION: 
 
Move to recommend the Governing Body approve the assigned zoning for land south of Storey 
Boulevard, east of Powderhouse Road, North of Dell Range Boulevard, and west of Ridge Road to AG 
Agricultural and MR Medium-density Residential as shown in the attached zone change map, noting that 
the project meets the review criteria for approval. 
 
APPLICABLE CITY CODE SECTION(S) AND PLANS: 
 
• 
UDC 2.2.8 Assigned Zoning 
 
BACKGROUND: 
The City is annexing parcels that are 100% surrounded by current City Limits as part of Councils goals to annex 
county pockets. Annexation of a parcel requires a city zone to be established.  
 
ANALYSIS OF APPLICATION CONFORMANCE WITH REVIEW CRITERIA: 
 
Should the Planning Commission wish to recommend approval of the Zoning Map Amendment, the 
Commission must make the following findings required in UDC 2.2.1.d. A detailed analysis is below: 
 
1. The proposed zoning assignment is the most analogous City zone to the current zone 
when taking into consideration allowed uses, current uses, or surrounding City zones. 
Zoning shall be assigned a City zone based on: 
a. Permitted uses, 
b. Uses that could reasonably be anticipated for conditional use approval 
under the current zoning, 
c. Current uses, or 
d. Surrounding City zoning, 
as deemed most suitable for the property being assigned a zone in terms of allowing 
the landowner to continue the current use of the land while respecting the current and 
anticipated uses of surrounding property. 
 
Through the reviewing of the County Pockets, the assigned zoning is fairly straightforward. 
 
Pocket A 
 
Pocket A is currently zoned LU – Land Use within the County. This pocket has a variety of City zone 
districts that surround it. There is commercial zoning to the south and mainly residential zoning to the 
east and west. To the north is County LU. This pocket is very large and a majority is currently under 
the ownership of one family. There is one parcel owned by the City and one by the School District. 
The land is currently used for grazing and is all vacant. Staff is unsure how this area will develop and 
due to the size and current use, Staff believes that the AG – Agricultural zone is the most analogous 
zone for this pocket. The AG zone is also a holding zone which will allow the land to be rezoned more 
easily in the future as the land develops.  
 

PUDC-25-258 
 
 
January 5, 2026 
3 | P a g e 
 
 
Pocket B 
 
Pocket B is currently zoned LU – Land Use within the County. There are three different parcels in this 
pocket that are all under the same ownership. These parcels are currently vacant. They are surrounded 
by residential and public zoning. To the west, northwest, and southwest, the surrounding land is 
zoned NR-3 – Neighborhood Residential-High Density. The other zoning to the north if NR-2 – 
Neighborhood Residential-Medium Density. The zoning to the southeast is MR- Medium-density 
Residential. P – Public zoning is to the southwest and northeast. Two of the parcels are designated 
Urban Residential by the future land use map and the parcel along Converse is designated Mixed-use 
Residential by the future land use map. More than likely, these parcels will develop with some type of 
residential use. Based on this information Staff believes that the MR – Medium-density Residential 
zone is the most analogous zone for this pocket. Although NR-3 and NR-2 zoning make up a majority 
of the adjacent zoning, Staff does not believe a higher-intensity residential zone is appropriate for the 
assigned zoning and should the developer of the land wish to upzone to a higher intensity residential 
zone, they can go through the Zone Change process which would review the higher-intensity zone on 
its merits and  require neighbor notification.  
 
Pocket C 
 
Pocket C is currently zoned LU – Land Use within the County. This pocket is surrounded by MR – 
Medium-density Residential on all sides and has a little bit of LR – Low-density Residential to the 
southwest. The land is currently vacant. Surrounding land uses are single-family residential on three 
sides and vacant land to the north. More than likely, this parcel will develop with some type of 
residential use. Based on this information Staff believes that the MR – Medium-density Residential 
zone is the most analogous zone for this pocket.  
 
This assigned zoning complies with review criterion 1. 
 
STAFF REVIEW: 
 
Through the review process, there were no comments.  
 
CONCLUSION: 
 
Staff is satisfied that the application meets the review criteria set forth in UDC 2.2.8.(d). 
 
ALTERNATIVES: 
 
1. Recommend approval of the Assigned Zoning as submitted by the applicant (Staff 
Recommendation).  
2. Recommend approval of the Assigned Zoning subject to conditions or modifications.  
3. Recommend denial of the Assigned Zoning based on findings of denial. The Planning 
Commission must conclude that the project does not meet all the review criteria. As part of the 
motion, findings for denial must be stated. 
4. Postpone the Assigned Zoning until issues identified during the meeting can be resolved. 
 
 
 

PUDC-25-258 
 
 
January 5, 2026 
4 | P a g e 
 
 
General Information Regarding Alternatives: 
 
If the Commission acts contrary to staff’s recommendation or recommends conditions of approval, the 
Commission shall cite specific reasons (based on the review criteria) for their recommendation. 
 
STAFF RECOMMENDATION: 
 
Staff recommends approval of the assigned zoning for zoning for land south of Storey Boulevard, east of 
Powderhouse Road, North of Dell Range Boulevard, and west of Ridge Road to AG Agricultural for pocket 
A and MR Medium-density Residential for pockets B and C as shown in the attached zone change map, 
noting that the project meets the review criteria for approval.

MEMO 
To: 
Honorable Mayor Patrick Collins and Members of the City Council 
From: 
Seth Lloyd, AICP - Senior Planner 
Subject: PLTA-25-10 Amending UDC 5.7.3 and 1.4.4 regarding Accessory Dwelling Units  
Date: 
January 12, 2026 
______________________________________________________________________________________________________________ 
Background  
The item for consideration is a UDC Text Amendment. The substance of this amendment was initiated 
by Councilman Mark Moody, but the language was crafted by Staff and modified by the Planning 
Commission. This is approved by Ordinance.    
The proposed Governing Body review schedule is as follows: 
•
Introduction and Referral 1/12/2026
•
Public Services Committee 1/20/2026
•
Second reading
1/26/2026 
•
Public Services Committee 2/2/2026
•
Third and final reading
2/9/2026 
Planning Commission Recommendation  
The Planning Commission heard this item at their January 5, 2026 meeting and voted 5-0 to 
recommend the Governing Body approve the proposed text amendment with the following change: 
•
1.4.4.a “Accessory Dwelling” changed to clarify the list of ADU options were examples, not an
exhaustive list.
Planning Commission’s proposed changes are reflected in the attached ordinance. 
The Planning Commission Staff report is attached and has not been changed (Except that areas 
changed by the Planning Commission have been noted in green to attempt to reduce confusion when 
comparing the Planning Commission Staff Report to the Ordinance).  
Public Comment 
As of the creation of this memo (January 7, 2026), Staff has received multiple comments 
from the public on this project in the form of letters (attached). The project page on the website has 
received 91 views and no comments.

ORDINANCE NO. ________ 
 
ENTITLED: “AN ORDINANCE AMENDING 5.7.3 ACCESSORY DWELLING UNITS 
OF THE CHEYENNE UNIFIED DEVELOPMENT CODE TO REMOVE 
OWNER-OCCUPANCY AND ADJUST PARKING REQUIREMENTS FOR 
ACCESSORY 
DWELLINGS, 
AND 
MAKING 
CONFORMING 
AMENDMENTS 
TO 
THE 
DEFINITIONS 
OF 
AN 
ACCESSORY 
DWELLING 
AND 
SEMI-ATTACHED 
DWELLING 
UNDER 
1.4.4 
DESCRIPTIONS OF USES OF LAND AND BUILDINGS.” 
 
 
BE IT ORDAINED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE, 
WYOMING: 
 
Section 1.  That the Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne finds that the text amendment 
to the Cheyenne Unified Development Code (UDC) that is the subject of this ordinance meets the 
review criteria specified in Section 2.4.1.d of the UDC. 
 
Section 2.  That on January 5, 2025, the Planning Commission conducted a public hearing, 
reviewed the proposed text amendment, and recommended approval of said text amendment. 
 
Section 3.  That 5.7.3 Accessory Dwelling Units, of 5.7 Specific Use Standards, of 
Article 5 Zoning Regulations, of the Cheyenne Unified Development Code is hereby amended to 
read: 
 
5.7.3 Accessory Dwelling Units 
 
In districts where accessory dwelling units are allowed as permitted, administrative or conditional 
uses, they shall meet the following standards in addition to all other lot and building standards for 
the district. 
 
a. The accessory dwelling unit may be in the principal building or any permitted accessory 
building, however all applicable lot and building type standards for the district shall apply. 
b. Only one accessory dwelling unit is permitted per detached one-unit dwelling lot in any zoning 
district where accessory dwellings are allowed as permitted, administrative or conditional uses. 
Accessory dwelling units are prohibited on lots with multi-dwelling buildings, attached 
dwellings or semi-attached dwellings. 
c. The property owner must occupy either the principal unit or the accessory dwelling unit as 
their permanent residence. 
dc. After approval by the Development Office Director of an accessory dwelling unit pursuant to 
subsection (b) of this section, the property owner will record with the Laramie County real 
estate office a “Notice of ADU Regulations” that shall contain a legal description of the subject 
property. The “Notice of ADU Regulations” shall be in a form approved by the Development 
Office Director prior to filling. 
ed. In no case shall an accessory dwelling unit be more than 40 percent of the principal dwelling 
unit up to a maximum of 1,200 square feet. This limitation is in addition to all other lot and 
10:00 am, Jan 07, 2026

Page 2 of 3 
 
building standards applicable in the district. Square footage calculations, as contained herein, 
exclude any related garage, porch, or similar area. 
fe. The unit size limitations shall not apply to basement apartments. The size of a basement 
apartment may occupy the entire floor area of the existing basement. 
gf. The accessory dwelling unit shall not include any design elements on principal or accessory 
buildings that alter the character of the lot, building type or district in general. 
hg. An accessory building used for an Accessory Dwelling Unit shall be designed to preserve or 
complement the architectural design, style and appearance of the principal building. 
ih. One off-street parking space shall be provided in addition to that which is required for the 
principal dwelling, and it shall be located on a discrete portion of the lot. 
 
Section 4.  That 1.4.4 Descriptions of Uses of Land and Buildings, of 1.4 Interpretation, of 
Article 1 General Provisions, of the Cheyenne Unified Development Code is hereby amended, 
with the entire text of the section, as amended, set forth in full in Attachment A pursuant to Wyo. 
Stat. § 15-1-117, which attachment is incorporated herein by this reference and shall control in the 
event of any inconsistency. The amendments adopted by Attachment A are limited to the following 
residential use definitions: 
 
Semi-attached Dwelling (“Duplex”).  A Residential use in a detached building designed as two 
principal dwelling units. It may be designed as a “paired house,” matching the appearance of 
a house, or it may be designed as a “flat-over-flat,” matching the appearance of a small 
apartment building or townhouse. A duplex is may be situated on its own fee simple lot, but 
each unit of a side-by-side paired house may shall meet all requirements to be platted on an 
two individual lot if party walls are used lots. Two units in the same building in the form of 
“flat-over-flat” duplex shall be considered an Accessory Dwelling, not a Semi-attached 
Dwelling. 
 
Accessory Dwelling.  A separate dwelling unit that is associated with, incidental to, and 
subordinate to another dwelling unit as the principal use, and is located on the same lot as the 
principal dwelling (commonly referred to as “In-laws Quarters” “Basement Apartment” or 
“Garage Apartment”). The Accessory Dwelling may take forms such as a stand-alone unit in 
an accessory building, a small unit added onto the principal building, or a “flat-over-flat” 
duplex. 
 
Section 5.  Sections, paragraphs, sentences, clauses, and phrases of this ordinance are 
severable. If any section, paragraph, sentence, clause, or phrase of this ordinance is declared 
unconstitutional or otherwise unlawful, such declaration shall not affect any of the remaining 
sections, paragraphs, sentences, clauses, and phrases. 
 
 
Section 6.  This ordinance shall be in full force and effect upon approval and publication.
