Technical corrections
CONCLUSION:
Staff analysis is that the application meets the review criteria and therefore recommends approval of the 
expedited plat application with the following condition:

2648).
ALTERNATIVES:

Recommend approval of the expedited preliminary plat, forwarding the Final Plat to the
Governing Body.

conditions of approval be prepared and presented to the Governing Body.

preliminary plat to the Governing Body.
4.

forward the preliminary plat to the Governing Body.

General Information Regarding Alternatives:
The Planning Commission may modify staff’s recommended conditions, delete conditions, add conditions, 
or decide contrary to staff’s recommendation. In the event conditions are modified, the conditions shall 
relate to applicable review criteria. In the event that the Commission acts contrary to staff’s 
recommendation or modifies conditions of approval, the Commission shall cite specific reasons (based on 
the review criteria) for approval or denial.
STAFF RECOMMENDATION:
Staff recommends approval of the Preliminary Plat, advancing the Final Plat to the Governing Body with 
the following condition:
1.
Update the owners name in the dedication statement to Woolly Bugger LLC (Bk 2786 Pg 
2648).

# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2026-01-06_TotalExtraction_42584169.pdf
Planning and Development Office 
2101 O’Neil Avenue, Suite 202, Cheyenne WY 82001 
(Phone) 307-637-6282   
(Fax) 307-637-6366 
 
 
 
 
MEMO 
To: 
Honorable Mayor Patrick Collins and Members of the City Council 
From: 
Connor White, Planner II 
Subject: County Pocket Annexation #10 
Date: 
January 12, 2026 
 
Background:  
 
The item for consideration is a resolution to allow the City to proceed with a City-initiated annexation. 
Annexation of County pockets is a 2022 City Council priority.  A “County pocket” is an area of 
unincorporated land 100% surrounded by the City of Cheyenne.   
 
The proposed Governing Body review schedule is as follows: 
• 
Introduction and referral  
January 12, 2026 
• 
Public Services Committee  
January 20, 2026 
• 
Final consideration 
 
January 26, 2026 
 
This annexation is comprised of 16 properties 100% surrounded by the City (See attached map). Of 
the 16 parcels, most are developed with residential, and a few are commercial / industrial. The City 
has maintained contact with the County Planning Office to coordinate any pending permits or 
development reviews.  Currently there are no active permits or development specific to the 
properties proposed for annexation.   
 
Annexation also requires establishing a City zone. Staff has created a tentative timeline for the 
annexation and establishment of City zoning districts for the subject properties. Zone districts will be 
established on the assigned zoning process of Unified Development Code (UDC) section 2.2.8. In the 
event of split zoning (where two zone districts divide an existing tract of land), the City intends to 
apply the more restrictive zone unless extenuating circumstances exist.   
 
 
 
  
  
  
  
  
  
  
  Planning and Development Department 
2101 O’Neil Avenue, Suite 202, Cheyenne WY 82001 
(Phone) 307-637-6282 (Fax) 307-637-6366

RESOLUTION NO. __________ 
 
ENTITLED: “A RESOLUTION CERTIFYING COMPLIANCE WITH WYOMING 
STATUTE § 15-1-402 FOR THE CITY-INITIATED ANNEXATION OF 
VARIOUS TRACTS OF LAND COMPLETELY SURROUNDED BY 
CURRENT CITY LIMITS SITUATE IN WESTERN AND SOUTHERN 
CHEYENNE, GENERALLY LOCATED EAST OF I-25, SOUTH OF THE 
INTERSECTION OF WESTLAND ROAD AND MISSILE DRIVE, NORTH 
OF WEST AND EAST ALLISON ROAD AND EAST OF AND ADJACENT 
TO NORTH COLLEGE DRIVE.” 
 
WHEREAS, the City of Cheyenne desires to initiate annexation proceedings for certain 
tracts of land and rights-of-way located in western and southern Cheyenne, comprising of 
approximately 48.38 acres, more or less, as depicted in Exhibit A, which is attached hereto and 
incorporated herein by this reference; 
 
WHEREAS, Wyoming Statute § 15-1-404 specifies the requirements for initiating 
annexation proceedings by cities and towns and requires that a resolution be adopted by the 
Governing Body certifying compliance with Wyoming Statute § 15-1-402; 
 
WHEREAS, based on reasonable evidence, the Governing Body has determined that the 
territory to be annexed complies with Wyoming Statute § 15-1-402 and meets the following 
criteria found in Wyoming Statute § 15-1-402(a): 
 
(i)  The annexation of the area is for the protection of the health, safety and welfare of the 
persons residing in the area and in the City of Cheyenne;  
 
(ii)  The urban development of the area sought to be annexed would constitute a natural, 
geographical, economical and social part of the City of Cheyenne;  
 
(iii)  The areas sought to be annexed is a logical and feasible addition to the City of 
Cheyenne and the extension of basic and other services customarily available to residents 
of the city or town shall, within reason, be available to the area proposed to be annexed; 
 
(iv)  The area sought to be annexed is contiguous with or adjacent to the City of Cheyenne;  
 
(v)  The Governing Body is prepared to issue one (1) or more franchises as necessary to 
serve the annexed area pursuant to Wyoming Statute § 15-1-410; and 
 
(vi)  The City of Cheyenne is prepared to, not less than twenty (20) business days prior to 
the public hearing required by Wyoming Statute § 15-1-405(a), send by certified mail to 
all landowners and affected public utilities within the territory a summary of the annexation 
report as required under Wyoming Statute § 15-1-402(c) and notice of the time, date and 
location of the public hearing required by Wyoming Statute § 15-1-405(a). 
12:43 pm, Dec 29, 2025

Page 2 of 2 
 
WHEREAS, the City of Cheyenne has created an annexation report, which contains, at a 
minimum, the required information from Wyoming Statute § 15-1-402(c); 
 
WHEREAS, the City of Cheyenne shall prepare for each landowner and affected public 
utility so requesting in writing, the estimated cost of infrastructure improvements required of the 
landowner and affected public utility related to the annexation pursuant to Wyoming Statute 
§ 15-1-402(e); 
 
WHEREAS, the annexation of this territory will not create an area which is situated entirely 
within the boundaries of the City of Cheyenne, following criteria found in Wyoming Statute 
§ 15-1-406(d); and 
 
WHEREAS, the Governing Body finds the territory to be annexed does not meet the 
criteria specified under Wyoming Statute § 15-1-412 and thus does not require the written consent 
of the owner of the territory before being annexed by the City of Cheyenne.  
 
 
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY 
OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING, that the territory as shown in Exhibit A, complies with Wyoming 
Statute §15-1-402, and that the procedures outlined in Wyoming Statute § 15-1-405 and Wyoming 
Statute § 15-1-406 shall hereby be initiated. 
 
 
PRESENTED, READ AND ADOPTED THIS _______ DAY OF __________________, 
2026.

Patrick Collins, Mayor

RESOLUTION NO. __________ 
 
ENTITLED: “A RESOLUTION AUTHORIZING THE SUBMISSION OF AN APPLICATION 
BY THE CITY OF CHEYENNE FOR STATE FUNDING FOR THE OFFICE OF 
STATE LANDS AND INVESTMENTS UNMET HOUSING GRANT.” 
 
 
WHEREAS, the Governing Body for the City of Cheyenne desires to participate in the Unmet 
Housing Grant Program administered by the Wyoming Office of State Lands and Investments (OSLI), to 
assist in funding the construction of twelve units of housing for low Area Median Income (AMI) 
participants transitioning from group housing to independent living in the Magic City Enterprise program 
for intellectually and developmentally disabled adults (the Project); 
 
 
WHEREAS, the Governing Body recognizes the need for the Project; 
 
 
WHEREAS, the Unmet Housing Grant program requires that criteria be met as described in the 
application, and the City of Cheyenne agrees to ensure satisfaction of all requirements;  
 
 
WHEREAS, if any of the disbursed grant funds are later deemed to not comply with the OSLI 
criteria, Wyoming Statutes overseeing the use of State of Wyoming funds, and Article 16, § 6 of the 
Wyoming Constitution, the grant applicant agrees to repay the ineligible grant funds within fifteen (15) 
days of such finding to the OSLI; and 
 
 
WHEREAS, the Governing Body intends that a request be submitted for a grant of $1,000,000 
from Unmet Housing Grant program for the cost of the construction of the proposed Project. 
 
 
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY OF 
CHEYENNE, WYOMING, that an application be submitted on behalf of the City of Cheyenne requesting 
one million dollars ($1,000,000.00) in Unmet Housing Grant Program funding from the Office of State 
Lands and Investments, for consideration to assist in funding for the Project, and that the Mayor and City 
Clerk are authorized to execute all documents associated with the grant application; and 
 
 
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED, that Elizabeth Finochiaro, Economic Resource Administrator for 
the City of Cheyenne, is hereby designated as the Project Administrator for the City to act on all matters 
relating to this funding application and award. 
 
 
PRESENTED, READ AND ADOPTED THIS _______ DAY OF __________________, 2026. 
 
 
__________________________________________  
Patrick Collins, Mayor 
 
(SEAL)

RESOLUTION NO. __________ 
 
ENTITLED: “A RESOLUTION AUTHORIZING THE SUBMISSION OF AN APPLICATION 
BY THE CITY OF CHEYENNE FOR STATE FUNDING THROUGH THE 
OFFICE OF STATE LANDS AND INVESTMENTS FOR A RODEO GROUNDS 
PEDESTRIAN 
TRANSPORTATION 
INFRASTRUCTURE 
GRANT, 
AND 
AUTHORIZING THE EXECUTION AND SUBMITTAL OF ALL NECESSARY 
DOCUMENTS IN SUPPORT OF THE APPLICATION.” 
 
WHEREAS, during the 2023 General Session of the Wyoming Legislature, as part of the 
State-funded capital construction bill, Original Senate File 146 (2023 Wyo. Sess. Ch. 187), the Legislature 
appropriated four million dollars ($4,000,000.00) to the Wyoming Office of State Lands and Investments 
(“OSLI”) for the purpose of providing grants for infrastructure related to qualifying special events 
conducted on the grounds of any indoor or outdoor rodeo facility with a total seating capacity of not less 
than seven thousand five hundred (7,500) (hereafter referred to as the “Rodeo Grounds Pedestrian 
Transportation Infrastructure Grant Program” or the “Program”); 
 
WHEREAS, under 2023 Wyo. Sess. Ch. 187, grant recipients under the Program are to be selected 
by the State Loan and Investment Board, and any grant awarded is subject to the following conditions: (i) 
the grant may only be awarded for infrastructure projects that create or enhance pedestrian transportation 
infrastructure in order to increase safety and avoid pedestrian and vehicle interactions, collisions, and 
conflicts; and (ii) expenditure of grant funds by a recipient is conditioned upon a matching contribution 
of not less than one dollar ($1.00) from any other source, including in-kind contributions, for every one 
dollar ($1.00) of grant funds received; 
 
WHEREAS, the appropriation authorized by 2023 Wyo. Sess. Ch. 187 reverts on June 30, 2026; 
 
WHEREAS, OSLI issued notice announcing the solicitation of grant proposals for the Rodeo 
Grounds Pedestrian Transportation Infrastructure Grant Program, as authorized under 2023 Wyo. Sess. 
Ch. 187, with a submission deadline of February 9, 2026; 
 
WHEREAS, the Governing Body for the City of Cheyenne desires to participate in the Program 
administered by OSLI to assist in funding the construction of a fully enclosed prefabricated truss 
pedestrian and bicycle bridge between the proposed Park-N-Ride lot and Frontier Park, spanning Interstate 
25 and Hynds Boulevard (the “Project”); 
 
WHEREAS, the Governing Body recognizes the need for the Project to enhance pedestrian safety 
and reduce conflicts between vehicles and pedestrians during special events at Frontier Park; 
 
WHEREAS, the City of Cheyenne intends to request grant funding in the amount of four million 
dollars ($4,000,000.00) from OSLI for the Project, and further intends to provide a local match in the 
amount of one million dollars ($1,000,000.00) toward the cost of construction of the Project, consistent 
with the grant program requirements; and 
 
WHEREAS, the City of Cheyenne understands and agrees that compliance with all applicable 
OSLI criteria, Wyoming statutes governing the use of State of Wyoming funds, and Article 16, Section 6 
of the Wyoming Constitution is required, and further understands that any grant funds later determined to 
be ineligible must be repaid to OSLI within fifteen (15) days of such determination. 
 
5:57 pm, Jan 20, 2026

 
Page 2 of 2 
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY OF 
CHEYENNE, WYOMING, that an application be submitted on behalf of the City of Cheyenne requesting 
four million dollars ($4,000,000.00) in Rodeo Grounds Pedestrian Transportation Infrastructure Grant 
Program funding from the Office of State Lands and Investments to assist in funding for the Project; 
 
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED, that the Mayor of the City of Cheyenne is hereby authorized to 
execute, submit, and deliver the grant application, together with any and all certifications, assurances, 
supporting materials, amendments, and other information that may be necessary or reasonably required 
by OSLI or the State Loan and Investment Board in connection with the application and any resulting 
grant award; 
 
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED, that the Mayor is further authorized to execute any ancillary 
documents related to the administration of the grant application and, if awarded, the grant itself, subject 
to compliance with applicable law; and 
 
BE IT FINALLY RESOLVED, that Elizabeth Finochiaro, Economic Resource Administrator for 
the City of Cheyenne, is hereby designated as the Project Administrator and authorized to act on behalf 
of the City on matters related to preparation, submission, and administration of the grant application and 
any resulting award. 
 
 
PRESENTED, READ AND ADOPTED THIS _______ DAY OF __________________, 
2026. 
 
 
__________________________________________  
Patrick Collins, Mayor

CITY OF CHEYENNE PLANNING COMMISSION

STAFF REPORT

lots.

PlanCheyenne
BACKGROUND:
The applicant is requesting approval of a preliminary plat for roughly 3.17 acres of land. The proposed plat 
would create three lots, two of which have existing single-family residences on them. The applicant had 
submitted for expedited review, meaning the final plat had also been submitted. Through staff’s review, there 
were several issues that arose, such as ownership and the proposed street vacation. Due to these issues, staff is 
not processing this application as an expedited plat and only the preliminary plat is moving forward at this 
time. 
SURROUNDING LAND USE AND ZONING:
ANALYSIS OF APPLICATION CONFORMANCE WITH REVIEW CRITERIA:
Should the Planning Commission wish to recommend approval of the Preliminary Plat, the Commission
must make the following findings required in UDC 2.1.2.d – a detailed analysis is below:
1) The application is in accordance with the development policies and physical 
patterns identified in the Comprehensive Plan. (UDC 2.1.2.d.1)
Future Land Use 
Designation

PUDC-25-254

3 | P a g e
On the future land use map found in PlanCheyenne, the subject land and surrounding area has 
been designated as Urban Residential. The Urban Residential designation encourages a range of 
residential uses. The proposed plat creates two lots for the existing single-family homes and a 
third lot for residential development. 
Foundation 1 of PlanCheyenne encourages community growth. This development is in response 
to market demands and is within the Urban Service Boundary. The development patterns 
proposed by this plat are compatible with the designations found on the future land use map.
The application complies with this review criterion. 
2) The application reflects sound planning and urban design principles with respect to 
existing and potential future development in the vicinity, including consideration 
of current and future streets, open spaces, blocks and lots, and public utilities or 
facilities. (UDC 2.1.2.d.2)
The proposed plat subdivides one lot into two and also proposes vacating right-of-way and 
platting that into the adjacent parcels. With the proposed vacation, staff believes that the entirety 
of Frank Court needs to be vacated and not just a portion. A partial vacation creates a street 
section that does not meet UDC standards and is not a benefit. Vacation with a plating action 
requires all owners adjacent to the right-of-way to sign on the plat application. Not all owners 
with interest in the proposed-to-be-vacated rights-of-way have signed on the plat.
The application complies with this review criterion, with conditions.  
3) The division of land meets the purposes, intent, and all applicable standards in 
Article 4. (UDC 2.1.2.d.3)
Community Facility Fees (4.2):
For fees based on acreage, the area of the plat is pro-rated to the nearest tenth of an acre. 
Lands reserved as unbuildable (for right-of-way, detention, parks, remainder lots, etc), are 
exempt from fees. Park fees are calculated at $700 per acre. All fees are due at the time of 
building permit.
Per UDC Section 4.2.5, Table 4-2, Enhancement Fees are payable at the issuance of a building 
permit for each structure. The Park Enhancement Fee for residential is $400 per dwelling unit.
Transportation Network (4.3):
This plat proposes vacating portions of Frank Court via the platting action. The proposed 
vacation by the applicant only vacates half the street. Staff believes that if any portion of 
Frank Court is to be vacated, the entire street should be vacated. Staff does not support a 
partial vacation. Full vacation may be done by including all owners in the plat or by the City 
initiating a vacation ordinance process.
This plat includes the east-west section of Frank Court which at this time has some ownership 
and vacation questions that surround it. There are issues with the resolution that is referenced 
on the plat and more research may be needed to determine if this section has been vacated 
and who actually owns it. 

PUDC-25-254
January 5, 2026
4 | P a g e
Although this plat proposes vacating streets, the streets to be vacated are undeveloped and 
would not provide meaningful street connections even if built. Due to Dry Creek, block 
connectivity is limited in this area and reducing block size by extending the current streets or 
by creating new streets is not reasonable. Staff recommends granting an exception to block 
size.
Open Space (4.4):
Open space is required at 750sqft per unit or 8% of the gross development area, whichever is 
less. Due to the nature of the proposed development pattern, open space will be taken care 
of with future development actions.
Required Improvements (4.5):
Utilities are available within this area already. The applicant needs to provide information that 
future connections can be made should any of the lots need to connect to City water and 
sewer. Should lot 1 ever need water and sewer, easements may be needed and are not 
provided at this time. 
Public Safety Fees (4.6):
Public Safety Fees are required at the time of construction of any residential building on any 
of the proposed lots. Per UDC Section 4.6.3.b, Public Safety Fees are payable at the issuance 
of a building permit for each structure. 
The application complies with this review criterion, with conditions.  
4) Any phasing proposed in the application is clearly indicated and demonstrates a 
logical and well-planned development pattern. This may include consideration of 
amenities and public facilities of the subdivision within each phase compared to 
the anticipated timing or the likelihood of future phases being constructed as 
planned. (UDC 2.1.2.d.4)
No specific phasing is proposed with this project.
Review criterion not applicable.  
5) The impacts identified in specific studies or technical reports that were prepared or 
requested to be prepared in association with the application, and whether the 
findings and recommendations of these reports, or any modifications or conditions 
on approval of the application, can mitigate these impacts. (UDC 2.1.2.d.5)
Drainage:
A drainage worksheet was submitted and reviewed. There were no concerns with drainage at the 
plat level for the project as presented. 
Traffic:
A transportation worksheet was submitted and reviewed. There were no concerns with traffic at 
the plat level for the project as presented. 

PUDC-25-254
January 5, 2026
5 | P a g e
The application complies with this review criterion.
6) Lots proposed for development shall be buildable lots according to the existing or 
proposed zoning district standards, result in design and development patterns that 
meet the Intent and Applicability statements of the existing or proposed zoning 
district, and not aggravate nonconforming setbacks or currently adopted building 
and fire code access and separation requirements. (UDC 2.1.2.d.6)
All lots proposed for development are buildable lots in this zone districts.
The application complies with this review criterion. 
7) All parcels not proposed as buildable lots and which are not dedicated to and 
accepted by the City shall have a clearly defined entity responsible for ongoing 
maintenance.  (UDC 2.1.2.d.7)
All proposed lots are proposed as buildable lots. The Plat does not propose any lots subject to 
this criterion.
Review criterion not applicable.
8) The application will not create any undue influence on existing or potential 
development for adjacent property considering the existing zoning and 
Comprehensive Plan policies for that property.  (UDC 2.1.2.d.8)
The previous development pattern in the area utilized wells and septic systems. The utility plan 
submitted by the applicant indicated that both houses are currently on wells and septic systems. If 
this is the case, the creation of the new Lot 3 may interfere with the locations and functions of 
these systems. Further information is needed to determine where existing facilities are prior to 
authorizing a final division of land. 
If existing wells and septic systems are abandoned and easements for utilities are extended, this 
plat will not create any undue influence on existing or potential development. Frank Court is an 
under utilized right-of-way that only servers one home. The surrounding lots are developed and 
have access to other rights-of-way. 
The application complies with this review criterion, with conditions.  
9) The application is not harmful to the public health, safety, and welfare. (UDC 
2.1.2.d.9)
Discussion on water, sewer, and utilities above are pertinent to the public health. City staff is
satisfied that this proposed plat would not otherwise adversely affect the public health, safety, or 
general welfare.
The application complies with this review criterion.  

PUDC-25-254
January 5, 2026
6 | P a g e
10)Remainder lot(s) shall be clearly identified on the preliminary plat and shall not be 
developed or proposed for development for purposes.  Remainder lot(s) shall be 
noted and described on all plats either graphically or by legal description. (UDC 
2.1.2.d.10)
Remainder lots are not proposed with this plat.
The application complies with this review criterion.  
CONFORMANCE WITH APPLICABLE PLANS:
The project is located in an area covered by the following plans:
x
PlanCheyenne
Conformance with PlanCheyenne is covered by review criterion 1.
PUBLIC NOTICE AND PUBLIC COMMENTS RECEIVED:
Although projects have public notice and the opportunity for public comments, projects are required to 
be approved or denied based on the review criteria (listed above). Public comments relevant to the review 
criteria may assist the review and discussion on the application.
The public was given notice of the project in the following ways:
-
Legal Notice in the Wyoming Tribune Eagle published on December 20, 2025
-
Posted Notice Evidence of posted notice was provided showing that the signs were posted on 
December 22, 2025.
-
Internet Posting on connect.cheyennecity.org on November 21, 2025
As of the publication of the Staff Report (December 29, 2025), Staff has received no inquiries or comments 
from the public on this project. The project page on the website has received 112 views and no 
comments.
STAFF REVIEW:
Through the review process, comments were received regarding the following issues:
1.
Technical correction
2.
Vacation of all of Frank Court
3.
Ownership questions
4.
Utility Plan
5.
Easements
CONCLUSION:
Staff analysis is that the application meets the review criteria and therefore recommends approval of the 
preliminary plat application with conditions.

PUDC-25-254
January 5, 2026
7 | P a g e
ALTERNATIVES:
1.
Recommend approval of the expedited preliminary plat, forwarding the Final Plat to the
Governing Body.
2.
Recommend approval of the preliminary plat with conditions and that a final plat addressing the 
conditions of approval be prepared and presented to the Governing Body. 
3.
Recommend approval of the preliminary plat as submitted by the applicant and forward the 
preliminary plat to the Governing Body.
4.
Recommend approval of the preliminary plat subject to staff’s recommended conditions and 
forward the preliminary plat to the Governing Body. 
5.
Recommend denial of the preliminary plat based on findings of denial. The Planning Commission 
must conclude that the project does not meet all the review criteria. As part of the motion, 
findings for denial must be stated.
6.
Postpone the project until issues identified during the meeting can be resolved.
General Information Regarding Alternatives:
The Planning Commission may modify staff’s recommended conditions, delete conditions, add conditions, 
or decide contrary to staff’s recommendation. In the event conditions are modified, the conditions shall 
relate to applicable review criteria. In the event that the Commission acts contrary to staff’s 
recommendation or modifies conditions of approval, the Commission shall cite specific reasons (based on 
the review criteria) for approval or denial.
STAFF RECOMMENDATION:
Staff recommends approval of the expedited Preliminary Plat, advancing the Final Plat to the Governing 
Body with the following exception and conditions:
1.
Exception to be granted:
a.
Block size exception due to natural features.
2.
Concurrent with Final Plat Submittal:
a.
Provide a vacation map to the City to initiate the full vacation of Frank Court, OR include 
all owners adjacent to Frank Court in the platting action.
b.
Clarify/verify owner and dedication information.
3.
On the Final Plat:
a.
Provide necessary easements. 
b.
Make minor technical corrections.

An expedited review is when both a preliminary and

together. To be eligible for expedited review the

determination by staff, the applicant may submit a
preliminary and final plat to the Planning
Commission.*

guaranteed.*
Written Statement*
Application to Replat the ExistinĈ Lots (7-9, B2, Brabetz (1955)) + RemaininĈ
Portions of Tracts 311 & 312, Sunnyside, 7th FilinĈ (1946)) to clarify and
reconcile record documents and consolidate the ownership parcels into lots
which correspond to built envirnoment. This application includes vacatinĈ
portions of un-utilized/unbuilt public R/W which has has been maintained
historically by the Owner/Applicant and has encroachinĈ structures, the
application retains a portion of the R/W containinĈ public facilites and/or
servinĈ neiĈhborinĈ properties or drainaĈe easements and an easement is
provided for existinĈ overhead transmission lines.

Review Criteria
The following review criteria are found in the UDC regarding a Preliminary Plat. Please
acknowledge the proposed plat meets each review criterion by checking the box.
The application is in accordance with the development
policies and physical patterns identified in the
Comprehensive Plan (2.1.2.d.1).*
The application reflects sound planning and urban
design principles with respect to existing and potential
future development in the vicinity, including
consideration of current and future streets, open
spaces, blocks and lots, and public utilities or facilities
(2.1.2.d.2).*

The division of land meets the purposes, intent, and all
applicable standards in Article 4 (2.1.2.d.3).*
Any phasing proposed in the application is clearly
indicated and demonstrates a logical and wellplanned
development pattern. This may include consideration
of amenities and public facilities of the subdivision
within each phase compared to the anticipated timing
or the likelihood of future phases being constructed as
planned (2.1.2.d.4).*
The impacts identified in specific studies or technical
reports that were prepared or requested to be
prepared in association with the application, and
whether the findings and recommendations of these
reports, or any modifications or conditions on
approval of the application, can mitigate these impacts
(2.1.2.d.5).*
Lots proposed for development shall be buildable lots
according to the existing or proposed zoning district
standards, result in site design and development
patterns that meet the Intent and Applicability
statements of the existing or proposed zoning district,
and not aggravate nonconforming setbacks or
currently adopted building and fire code access and
separation requirements (2.1.2.d.6).*
All parcels not proposed as buildable lots and which
are not dedicated to and accepted by the City shall
have a clearly defined entity responsible for ongoing
maintenance (2.1.2.d.7).*
The application will not create any undue influence on
existing or potential development for adjacent
property considering the existing zoning and
Comprehensive Plan policies for that property
(2.1.2.d.8).*
The application is not harmful to the public health,
safety, and welfare (2.1.2.d.9).*
Remainder lot(s) shall be clearly identified on the
preliminary plat and shall not be developed or
proposed for development for purposes. Remainder
lot(s) shall be noted and described on all plats either
graphically or by legal description (2.1.2.d.10).*
The following review criteria are found in the UDC regarding a Final Plat. Please
acknowledge the proposed plat meets each review criterion by checking the box.

The layout and design of the final plat is in substantial
compliance with the approved preliminary plat
considering the number of lots or parcels, street and
block layout, and access, and any deviations in the
final plat brings the application in further compliance
with the Comprehensive Plan (2.1.3.d.1).*
The application meets any conditions or modifications of the approved preliminary plat
(2.1.3.d.2). (Not applicable at time of application for an expedited plat)
The application meets the purposes, intent, and
applicable standards of Article 4 (2.1.3.d.3).*

# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2026-01-06_TotalExtraction_58418804.pdf
 
2101 O’Neil Ave. · Cheyenne · Wyoming 
Phone 307.638.4385 
www.plancheyenne.org 
 
 
MEMO 
 
TO: 
Honorable Mayor Patrick Collins and Members of the City Council 
FROM: 
Jennifer Corso – Senior Planner, Cheyenne MPO 
DATE: 
December 30, 2025 
SUBJECT: 
Professional Services Agreement for East Allison Road Corridor Study 
______________________________________________________________________________ 
 
The Cheyenne MPO is excited to begin the planning process to study the East Allison Rd 
Corridor.  
 
A Request for Qualifications was advertised on the PlanCheyenne website and the Wyoming 
Tribune Eagle on September 20, 2025. We received seven proposals. Stantec was selected as the 
best qualified. 
 
This contract is not-to-exceed the amount of $199,514.44. 
 
The East Allison Road Corridor Study will evaluate roadway, drainage, utility, and multimodal 
needs along East Allison Road in South Cheyenne. The study area extends from South Greeley 
Highway (US 85) to the existing west end of East Allison Road in the Niobrara Industrial Park, a 
total of approximately 0.9 miles. 
 
The study is divided into two phases, with Phase 1 advanced to ~30% design for greater 
accuracy at the US-85 gateway, and Phase 2 delivered at ~10% conceptual level with centerline 
survey. The study will also include a review of a potential roundabout at the East Allison Road & 
Avenue C intersection. 
 
Please reach out to me directly if you have any questions regarding this contract.  I can be reached at 637-
6280 and jcorso@cheyennempo.org.

PREAMBLE 
 
 
THIS AGREEMENT is made and entered into this _____ day of _____________, 2026, 
by and between the City of Cheyenne, Cheyenne Metropolitan Planning Organization, hereinafter 
referred to as the “MPO”, located at 2101 O’Neil Avenue, Room 304, Cheyenne, WY 82001, and 
Stantec Consulting Services Inc, hereinafter referred to as “Consultant”, located at 7000 
Yellowstone Rd Suite 230, Cheyenne, WY 82009. The City of Cheyenne is acting as the agent of 
the MPO for contract administration and as the MPO’s Fiscal Manager in accordance with Contract 
#6111. 
 
AGREEMENT 
In consideration of the mutual covenants and obligations herein expressed, it is agreed by 
and between the parties as follows: 
 
1. 
SCOPE OF WORK. The Consultant agrees to provide services as described for the above-
mentioned project, in accordance with the Scope of Work attached hereto as Exhibit A, 
incorporated by reference and expressly made a part hereof. 
 
2. 
TIME OF PERFORMANCE. The work included in this Scope of Work will be completed 
in accordance with the Project Schedule attached hereto as Exhibit B, incorporated by 
reference and expressly made a part hereof. Any extensions in the project schedule shall be 
agreed upon in writing by the parties. 
 
3. 
COMPENSATION. In consideration of the services to be performed pursuant to this 
Agreement, the Consultant will bill the MPO and the MPO agrees to pay Consultant a total 
cost-not-to-exceed amount of one hundred ninety-nine thousand five hundred fourteen 
dollars and 44 cents ($199,514.44), in accordance with the fee schedule provided in Exhibit 
C, which is attached to this Agreement, incorporated by reference, and expressly made a 
part hereof. The amount of all such payments shall be based upon the Consultant’s progress, 
verified by the MPO, in completing the work as described in Exhibits A, B, and C. Final 
payment shall be made following acceptance of the work by the MPO. Original quality 
reproductions of all designs, plans, reports, specifications, drawings, and other services 
rendered by the Consultant shall become the sole property of the MPO and shall be 
delivered to the MPO, immediately upon their preparation to the extent the MPO has 
satisfied its payment obligations to the Consultant. The MPO may withhold a portion of 
any payment otherwise due until any claims, disputes, or deficiencies related to the 
Consultant’s work are resolved.

A.

Federal General Provisions regarding breach of agreement consistent with 23 CFR 
172 and 2 CFR 200. These Regulations require written procedures to address 
contractual, legal, and administrative remedies including sanctions and penalties 
where consultants breach agreement terms, conditions, provisions, or obligations. 
For purposes of these written procedures, the Agreement terms, conditions, 
provisions, or obligations will be referred to as Agreement Services. 
 
Numerous Federal General Provisions will be administered using these written 
procedures. For purposes of administering consultant agreements, breach of 
agreement may result when analyzing a consultant’s professional services under 
any of the following Federal General Provisions:

D. 
Administrative Procedures, Sanctions, and Penalties.  Cheyenne MPO concerns 
with consultant performance and/or adherence to Agreement Services will most 
often be resolved through the coordination and resolution efforts as outline in 
SECTION EE – AGREEMENT ADMINISTRATION of this document. The 
authorized representative/ primary contact of the Cheyenne MPO should document 
all administrative issues and subsequent resolutions, from start to completion of the 
Agreement. 
 
There may be an occurrence when a cooperative and acceptable resolution cannot 
be reached between the Cheyenne MPO and the Consultant. At those occurrences, 
the Cheyenne MPO will typically make the determination that the Consultant has 
failed to perform Agreement-required acceptable work, has failed to progress in the 
performance of Agreement Services, or has not and will not comply with General 
Provisions. When that determination concludes that the Agreement Services cannot 
be completed and the Cheyenne MPO will incur additional cost, lost opportunity, 
or additional time to obtain the same or equal Agreement deliverables, the threshold 
for a material breach of agreement has been reached and will invoke SECTION 20, 

 
Professional Services Agreement 
for East Allison Road Corridor Study 
Page 19 of 21 
FEDERAL GENERAL PROVISIONS, SECTION 21 – BREACH OF 
AGREEMENT and the resultant remedies, including SECTION 20, FEDERAL 
GENERAL PROVISIONS, SECTION V – TERMINATION OF AGREEMENT. 
 
Any Cheyenne MPO determination that the Consultant has failed to perform 
Agreement-required acceptable work, has failed to progress in the performance of 
Agreement Services, or has not and will not comply with General Provisions will 
need to be supported by the Cheyenne MPO documentation of monitoring activities 
as allowed by SECTION 20, FEDERAL GENERAL PROVISIONS, SECTION N 
– MONITORING ACTIVITIES. Issues concerning the Consultant’s billing of 
allowable costs should be evaluated in accordance with SECTION 20, FEDERAL 
GENERAL PROVISIONS, SECTION B – ACCESS TO RECORDS. 
 
The threshold for a material breach of agreement requires that the Consultant has 
failed to perform Agreement Services and that the Cheyenne MPO has or will incur 
additional cost, lost opportunity, or additional time to obtain the same or equal 
Agreement deliverables. Both represent a high threshold to assure an acceptable 
outcome and, as a result, the Cheyenne MPO representative through monitoring 
activities must identify and document unresolved issues early in the Agreement, 
before either the Consultant or the Cheyenne MPO has incurred substantial cost or 
time. All unresolved issues should be promptly addressed, either reaching 
resolution, arriving at reasonable penalties/sanctions, or concluding breach of 
agreement with the resultant remedies, including SECTION 20, FEDERAL 
GENERAL PROVISIONS, SECTION V – TERMINATION OF AGREEMENT. 
 
Penalties and/or sanctions typically available to the Cheyenne MPO would be 
structured as 1) compensatory damages, 2) specific performance, or 3) termination. 
 
Damages, based on additional cost or time incurred by the Cheyenne MPO, could 
be quantified and pursued. Damages, based on lost opportunity incurred by the 
Cheyenne MPO, may be more difficult to quantify. Lost opportunity could include 
Cheyenne MPO delays in the delivery of supplemental work or successor 
agreements for work, or delay in the year of project construction and the related 
increased construction costs. Other lost opportunities may be identified and 
quantified. 
 
Specific performance would require the Consultant to pursue Agreement Services, 
with adjustment to allowable costs. Specific performance would be used as a 
remedy, either prior to or for breach of agreement, if the work required by the 
Agreement required special expertise, is an emergency, or is only available from a 
single or restricted number of firms. In those cases, damages would not suffice to 
place the Cheyenne MPO in as good a position as it would have been had the breach 
not occurred.

Termination of the Agreement is presented in SECTION 20, FEDERAL 
GENERAL PROVISIONS, SECTION V – TERMINATION OF AGREEMENT. 
The Cheyenne MPO may terminate the Agreement, and either pursue restitution or 
not pursue restitution. Restitution, as a remedy, means that the Cheyenne MPO is 
put back in the position it was in prior to the breach; without restitution, the 
Agreement is terminated with both the Consultant and the Cheyenne MPO no 
longer under any Agreement obligation.

Dear Christopher, 
Stantec is pleased to provide the Scope of Work associated with professional services to complete the 
above referenced project. Also see the attached AVI Proposal for additional Scope of Work associated with 
this project. 
BACKGROUND 
In support of the East Allison Road Reconstruction Project (South Greeley Highway to Avenue C) identified 
in the City’s 2026-2029 Transportation Improvement Program for fiscal year 2027, the Cheyenne MPO is 
conducting a corridor study to analyze multimodal mobility, traffic demand, safety, stormwater drainage, 
access management, and roadway infrastructure for the corridor.  
The corridor will be evaluated in three (3) segments which will each have distinct improvement strategies 
and will be addressed independently as part of the overall corridor strategy: 
Segment 1:  
East Allison Road from South Greely Highway (US 85) east to Avenue C. 
Segment 2:  
East Allsion Road from Avenue C east to the current west end of the improved Allison Road 
in Niobrara Industrial Park 
Segment 3:  
Existing Allison Road section from a cul-de-sac on the west end of Allison Road in the 
Niobrara Industrial Park, connecting to College Drive (WYO 212). Only the intersection of 
Allison Road and Energy Drive and Allsion Road and College Drive will be analyzed for LOS. 
EXISTING CONDITIONS ANALYSIS 
Stantec will review the relevant existing studies and development actions and make recommendations that 
incorporate and remain aligned with these studies. Plans, reports, and data that are relevant to this study 
include:  
• 
Existing traffic volumes 
• 
Existing operational data 
• 
Existing safety data (Crash data from the City and WYDOT) 
• 
Pavement conditions

•

STORMWATER DRAINAGE NEEDS 
Utilizing PCSWMM, Stantec will assess the existing surface drainage and flooding issues along the 
corridor. Stantec will use the information from this assessment to identify and recommend two (2) 
stormwater outfall locations to discharge into Allison Draw. The right-of-way impacts and construction 
feasibility for recommended drainage infrastructure will be addressed as part of this task. 
PUBLIC INVOLVEMENT 
We will develop and conduct a public involvement campaign involving residents, landowners, developers 
and project stakeholders to obtain public input. We will follow the Cheyenne MPO Public Participation Plan 
in addition to all items listed below.  
• 
Conduct stakeholder meetings to gain perspective and obtain feedback. 
• 
Look for Steering Committee involvement for guidance throughout the project. 
• 
Provide digital copies of the draft and final plans for review on the Cheyenne MPO webpage. 
• 
Hold public meetings during a minimum of two (2) phases of the project, early in the process, and 
after a preliminary report has been drafted for public involvement. As part of this process we will 
provide an open public comment period to solicit input both at the meeting and for a minimum of 
two (2) weeks thereafter. 
MEETING REQUIREMENTS 
1. Kickoff meeting with Project Steering Committee (virtual) 
2. Monthly coordination call with Project Steering Committee (virtual)

CONCEPTUAL ROADWAY DESIGN OPTIONS 
As part of this task Stantec will evaluate multimodal options such as sidewalk, bike lanes and shared-use 
paths, access management strategies, safety and capacity improvements, and drainage design options for 
surface conveyance and outfalls. Stantec assumes no more than two (2) alternatives associated with the 
conceptual road design task will be completed. 
Segment 1:  
30% preliminary design package to include roadway alignment, preliminary drainage and 
utility design, right-of-way strip maps, multimodal facilities and alternatives for the US-
85/East Allson Road intersection and the Avenue C/East Allison Road intersection. 
The 30% design package will include: 
- 
Proposed horizontal and vertical alignments (WB-67 Design Vehicle) 
- 
Proposed roadway typical sections 
- 
Grading quantities (unclassified excavation and topsoil) 
- 
Hydrologic Analysis (10 year – 2 hour storm & 100 year – 2 hour storm) and subsequent 
hydraulics design (10 year Minor Event & 100 year Major Event (operational evaluation)) 
- 
Deliverables for the 30% Design will be in 11” x 17” PDF format and include: 
Title Sheet 
Typical Roadway Sections 
Plan & Profile Sheets 
Storm drainage design sheets 
Preliminary Earthwork Quantities 
Traffic Tech Memo 
Drainage Tech Memo 
Segment 2:  
10% concept design package including centerline survey, conceptual roadway layout, 
multimodal connections, drainage strategy, and tie-ins to existing the Allison Road in the 
Niobrara Industrial Park area. 
The 10% design package will include:

Segment 3:  
Analyze the Level of Service (LOS) for the intersections of Allison Road and Energy Drive 
and Allison Road and College Drive. No design options will be completed for this section. 
PLANNING LEVEL COST ESTIMATES 
After the Concept Roadway Design Options are vetted, Stantec will complete planning level cost estimates 
for each option. Stantec will utilize bid tabs for recently completed projects in and around Cheyenne from 
both the City of Cheyenne and WYDOT. 
DELIVERABLES 
Specific deliverables are expected to include the following:  
1. Complete East Allison Road Corridor Study report with plans for each segment as discussed 
above.  
2. Displays for staff, steering and/or public meetings including maps, boards, handouts, surveys, and 
sign-in sheets. These will be available as electronic copies to the MPO to review at a minimum of 4 
business days prior to meetings. 
3. Summary of all public meetings and steering committee meetings to be produced within two weeks 
after closing the public survey for each event. 
4. A memo document will be included with every invoice, including a summary of work completed 
during that period, anticipated tasks for the next month and any impacts to the schedule. 
5. Electronic copies of all documents, images, photos, shapefiles, maps, draft and final plans. 
6. Two (2) hard copies of the completed plan. 
ASSUMPTIONS/CLARIFICATIONS: 
1. It is assumed that all advertisement fees and other associated advertisement costs will be invoiced 
directly to the Cheyenne MPO and is not included in our fee estimate provided for the above scope 
of work.

RE: 
PROPOSAL FOR THE EAST ALLISON ROAD CORRIDOR STUDY (UPDATED) 
Dear Ben, 
AVI is pleased to submit the following proposal for engineering, planning, and surveying 
services to assist Stantec in the delivery of a successful corridor study for the City of Cheyenne 
Metropolitan Planning Organization (MPO). AVI’s proposed scope of work and fee for the 
project are below and listed in the format included in the RFQ: 
PROJECT MANAGEMENT 
This task will include management and administrative items associated with AVI’s scope of the 
work including all necessary progress reports, updated project schedules, invoices, and general 
correspondence. This task will also include the AVI project manager’s attendance of the project 
kickoff meeting, monthly progress meetings (12 anticipated), external coordination meetings 
and presentation of the final study to the MPO Technical Advisory Committees and MPO Policy 
Committee.  
Fee for this Task – $9,740.48 
TASK 1: EXISTING CONDITIONS ANALYSIS 
Topographic Surveying & Utility Investigations – AVI will provide existing conditions 
surveying and mapping of the corridor in line with the MPO’s expected level of detail as listed in 
the RFQ.  
Segment 1: East Allison Rd from South Greeley Hwy (US 85) to Avenue C (approximately 0.55 
miles) – AVI will perform a detailed ground topographic survey of all existing ground 
features/topography from right of way line to right of way line and 50 feet in each direction at 
all intersections. Ground topography outside of the existing right of way will be supplemented 
by City/County aerial contours and incorporated into the ground survey data. Any aerial 
contours used as a supplement to the ground survey will extend 50 feet beyond the existing 
right of way lines.  In addition to the collection of ground features, AVI will call for 811 utility 
locates and utilize any available mapping data provided by buried utility providers necessary to 
provide utility mapping at a accuracy level of Quality Level C as defined by ASCE 38-22 Standard 
Guideline for Investigating and Documenting Existing Utilities.  
"EXHIBIT A"

 
East Allison Road Corridor Study
December 4, 2025 
Page 2 of 3 
25.189 
 
Segment 2: East Allison Rd from Avenue C to the west end of improved Allison Rd in the Niobrara 
Industrial Park (approximately 0.35 miles) – AVI will not complete any ground surveying thru this 
segment but will utilize City/County aerial mapping and maps provided by utility providers 
necessary to compile the existing conditions thru this segment. 
Segment 3: Existing East Allison Rd section from the cul-de-sac on the west end of Allison Rd in the 
Niobrara Industrial Park to College Dr (approximately 0.70 miles) – AVI will not complete any 
ground surveying thru this segment but will utilize City/County aerial mapping and maps 
provided by utility providers necessary to compile the existing conditions thru this segment. 
Once all survey data is collected/processed and combined with all available data provided by 
others, AVI will provide deliverable files in AutoCAD Civil 3D (latest release) that will include all 
existing ground conditions collected and compiled, all applicable aerial maps, and a point codes 
list of all surveyed features.  
Fee for this Task – $13,561.46 
TASK 2: STORMWATER DRAINAGE NEEDS 
AVI is not scoped to provide services as part of this Task.  
Fee for this Task – $0.00 
TASK 3: PUBLIC STAKEHOLDER ENGAGEMENT 
Public Open House Attendance – AVI will provide available technical staff to support Stantec 
during the two (2) planned public open houses. AVI will make available 2-3 staff members for 
each public open house and this effort will include one (1) preparatory informational meeting 
ahead of each open house for staff members in attendance.   
Fee for this Task – $9,866.14 
TASK 4: CONCEPTUAL ROADWAY DESIGN OPTIONS 
Existing Utility Improvement Options – In support of the conceptual roadway design efforts 
completed by Stantec, AVI will coordinate with existing utility providers (gas, electric, 
communication, water, sewer, ect…) on necessary relocation/improvement needs impacted with 
the preferred roadway design options and provide a narrative of recommendations in the final 
study.  
Fee for this Task – $3,486.08 
TASK 5: RIGHT OF WAY IDENTIFICATION AND PHASING 
Existing Right of Way Delineation – AVI will lay out existing right of way thru a combination of 
found surveyed boundary corners during the ground surveying efforts supplemented with 
information obtained from recorded plats along the limits of the corridor. In addition, AVI will 
also provide a narrative of recommended survey work to be completed along the corridor with 
future design implementation projects. 
"EXHIBIT A"

 
East Allison Road Corridor Study
December 4, 2025 
Page 3 of 3 
25.189 
 
Right of Way Phasing – In collaboration with the project steering committee and Stantec’s 
design team, AVI will provide right of way recommendations and phasing options along the 
corridor that will be implemented into the final study.  
Fee for this Task – $8,715.20 
TASK 6: PLANNING LEVEL COST ESTIMATES 
Utility Improvements and Right of Way Acquisitions Cost Estimates – Thru AVI’s proposed 
utility relocation/improvement scope and right of way acquisition/phasing efforts, AVI will 
provide planning level cost estimates to include with the overall project cost estimates.  
Fee for this Task – $1,021.68 
AVI’s Total Fee for this Project – $46,391.04 
CLOSURE 
AVI has reviewed the Subconsultant Agreement attached provided by Stantec and has no 
reservations or objections to the provisions outlined in said agreement.  
If you concur in all the foregoing and wish to direct us to proceed with the services, please have 
authorized persons enter pertinent information for Stantec in the Subconsultant Agreement 
ready for AVI to sign and execute. We will commence services only after we have received a fully 
executed agreement and a Notice to Proceed has been issued by Stantec. Fees and times stated 
in this Agreement are valid for sixty (60) days after the date of this letter. 
We appreciate the opportunity to provide professional services to Stantec and the Cheyenne 
MPO and are ready to begin work as soon as you are given the Notice to Proceed by the 
Cheyenne MPO which is anticipated sometime in early 2026. Please contact me if you have any 
questions. 
Respectfully Submitted
